- **No clipboard write exists, so no "Copy report" button — design around it.** LWJGL 2's `Sys` ships `getClipboard` only (verified in the shipped jar's constant pool); `TextFieldAPI` has paste-in (`setHandleCtrlV`) but no copy-out; `java.awt.Toolkit` is presumed blocked by the mod classloader (untested — spike only if a copy button is ever wanted, never on the critical path). `Sys.openURL` IS available: a dialog button can open the support thread (`file://` to the log folder is plausible on Windows but untested). Every support affordance therefore degrades to "here is the exact file and the exact string to search for."
- **Reference implementations, in reading order:** `starsector-fleethistory` (`BaseFleetHistoryIntelPlugin.java` + `FleetSummaryIntel.java`), Stellar Networks `uilib2/intel` (composable confirm/redraw contract), MagicLib (`InteractiveUIPanelPlugin.kt`, `MagicPaintjobManager.kt`), Industrial Evolution (in-dialog live rebuild; null-target retry loop in `BeaconAbilityPlugin.java`). TASC and Ashes of the Domain are rejected as models — both reach the core UI through MethodHandles over obfuscated classes, which the stability rubric forbids.

**Deferred issue this phase owns (2026-08-24, user decision):** observed live in the Phase 29 M1
smoke sessions — a host that loads its save before the guest connects **starts unpaused and plays on
alone**; the existing connect-time hold (`maybeHoldHostPausedUntilSessionReady`) covers the
handshake-in-progress window, not the nobody-connected-yet window, so the shared world advances
without the guest. Deliberately NOT patched with another interim pause source: the lobby's
force-pause-until-all-ready below is the real fix, and the first Steps item already reuses the
connect-pause hold for exactly this. Until Phase 21 lands, the workaround is host etiquette (pause
after loading until the guest appears).

**Scope (design decisions pinned by the 2026-08-26 research pass; these bullets are the spec):**

- **In-campaign lobby (`coop.ui.CoopLobbyDialog`).** Host loads the save → world force-paused → lobby interaction dialog: connected players, per-player ready state, the Phase 20 connection-doctor verdict (tier, external endpoint, RTT) rendered as dialog text instead of log lines, and a "Start" gated on all-ready. Guest sees the same lobby in read-mostly form after handshake. Unpause when everyone readies. (The pause machinery, input blocker, and rebroadcast-on-start all exist — this is choreography, not new sync.) Pinned decisions:
  - **Two-stage gate: all-ready arms the Start option, the host presses it.** The host is auto-ready — its ready IS the Start press (Phasmophobia's shape, the widely copied convention). **Never auto-start on all-ready:** Barotrauma shipped the exact trap this avoids (auto-start + non-revocable ready + a modal sub-dialog = an inescapable reopen loop, their issue #14894), and we have more modal surfaces to collide with than they do. **Ready is revocable** at any time before unpause.
  - **Host escape hatch from day one:** a confirm-guarded secondary option "Start anyway (guest not ready)". Every shipped all-ready gate without an override turns a ready-state bug into a rehost (Risk of Rain 2's deadlocked lobbies; Payday 2 players modded a force-start in). Cheap and safe here because the failure mode is just "guest mirrors a running world instead of a paused one."
  - **Roster: one row per player = name + state word + reason** ("Connecting…", "Syncing 3/5", "Ready", "Not ready", "Reconnecting 0:42", "Mod mismatch") — not colored dots (dots alone get mis-read; the state word carries the load). The blocking condition is named in the Start option's own label ("Waiting for <name>…" / "Start session") — explained waits read shorter than unexplained ones, and Stardew's "Waiting for players 1 of 4" is the shipped counter-example that works.
  - **Handshake progress = ~5 coarse named phases** (link established → versions matched → seed locked → snapshot applied → ready) as a step counter, not a percent bar (a bar pinned at 100% reads as hung — Factorio's join-stall complaint). Machine-level detail stays in the log. **Never a static frame >10 s:** tick an elapsed or bytes counter so "slow" is distinguishable from "dead."
  - **Ready-state lifecycle:** ready resets when the host changes a session-level setting (with a feed line saying why), NOT when a player joins; a disconnect during the lobby keeps the row and its ready value through the reconnect grace window, shown as "Reconnecting (m:ss)". No ready timeout ever auto-starts or auto-cancels — the correct non-response outcome here is "stay paused" (an AFK hint after ~2 min surfaces the host override, it does not fire it).
  - **On Start: a short (~3 s) visible countdown any player can cancel,** then the three-step release (dismiss + `setPaused(false)` + `hostPauseIntent` clear). Telegraphs the point of no return; costs nothing.
  - **The lobby renders purely from session state,** so reopening it is free and it doubles as the mid-session admit and reconnect surface: a guest connecting mid-session re-engages the hold (world pauses — Factorio 2.0 shipped exactly this as `server_auto_pause_on_join`, and the only objection there, public-server griefing, cannot occur in an invite-only session) and the same lobby opens with the new row. **One admission in the handshake at a time; a guest that drops mid-handshake has its partial state dropped and its row removed** (Nitrox's serialized join queue + explicit abandonment cleanup — two independent codebases hit state corruption from concurrent joins). v1 has one guest, but the rule is written now so Phase 27 inherits it.
- **Guest "connecting…" dialog** between socket connect and lobby admit: the same phase list the host sees, a Cancel that is always live and safe (a cancelled join must not disturb the host), and on failure it names the cause — version mismatch / host refused / timed out — never one generic string (Elden Ring Seamless Co-op's undifferentiated "no sessions found" is the support-load cautionary tale).
- **Desync dialogs — three, not one.** Hang off the *existing* loud-failure points only (seed-lock/fingerprint reject, `SESSION_RESUME_REJECT` once Phase 20 lands, version/mod handshake mismatch); no new desync *detection* is built in this phase. Each failure gets its own dialog (never one dialog with a swappable reason string — every detectable cause gets its own message, per the Microsoft error-message spec, and AoE2's single cause-less "out of sync" dialog is the ten-year cautionary tale). Shared rules:
  - **Structure: problem → cause → remedy, remedy above technical detail** (Fabric Loader's solution-then-"More details" ordering). Two verb-labeled buttons max (that is what `showConfirmDialog` gives; `cancel = null` for one). No "OK" button, no "error/failed/invalid" wording, no exclamation marks.
  - **No auto-retry.** All three failures are deterministic; Factorio shipped auto-reconnect-on-desync, capped it at 3, then deleted it in 0.16 ("a dialog with more information will be shown" instead). A retry button appears only when the specific reason is plausibly transient.
  - **Support pattern (no clipboard exists):** each dialog carries a short greppable code — `COOP-SEED`, `COOP-MODS`, `COOP-SESSION` — plus the log file name and exact search string inline in the body, and writes one single-line `[COOP-DOCTOR]` marker into the log carrying every field a helper needs, with `sessionId` as the correlation id (identical in host and guest logs, so a support thread with two pastes can be matched — Paradox's OOS-id insight). Optional button: `Sys.openURL` to the support thread. Unmapped reasons fall back to "Co-op session ended (<reason>)", never blank (Barotrauma's fallback pattern; Nitrox's empty-message bug is what it prevents).
  - **Seed-lock dialog:** show both fingerprints short and typeable (8 hex, hyphenated — Barotrauma's "Your MD5 / Server's MD5" shape) and the host's seed as literal text (it is the remedy, and the user cannot copy-paste it). Both legitimate remedies in likelihood order: new guest campaign from the host's seed, or load the coordinated saves. Hard block, no "join anyway."
  - **Mod-mismatch dialog:** a relative-labeled per-mod diff ("you have 2.7 / host has 2.8 / not installed / not on host — disable it"), each row carrying its own remedy verb (tModLoader's pattern); a dedicated sentence for the same-version-different-contents case (users don't believe it otherwise — Factorio wrote one); blame routed to the host when the host is the stale side ("Ask the host to update…", Don't Starve Together's pattern); list capped at ~8 rows with an overflow line naming the log (Forge caps at 10; uncapped lists have overflowed off-screen in shipped games). **No mod auto-download, ever** — every game that ships it has a trusted first-party portal or a "mods may contain malicious code" warning; we have neither. Gating stays strict equality (the existing `CoopHandshakeDiff`); a declared client-side-only allowlist is a recorded option if strict equality proves too brittle in the soak (RimWorld MP's strictest check is also its top false-positive source).
  - **Resume-reject dialog:** states the elapsed grace window as a number, with a distinct body per detectable reason (grace expired / host on a different campaign / slot taken); retry button only for the transient reasons.
- **Session stats page.** Extend `CoopSessionIntel` (Phase 20.6) with host-tallied per-session counters, broadcast on the existing snapshot cadence (`SESSION_STATS`), displayed via `createLargeDescription`. Cosmetic; accept divergence on edge cases. **Stat set corrected 2026-08-26** (the old "kills per player" premise was wrong — `BATTLE_RESULT` has no per-ship data, and per-player kill counts are also the single most contested stat in the coop-game corpus; Ghost Ship refused a damage counter for DRG as "too competitive for the co-op spirit"):
  - **Free on today's wire:** battles fought + battles won per player (`engagingPlayerId` + `outcome`), team-total ships destroyed, distance traveled per player, net worth + best single trade + markets traded with (`GUEST_SNAPSHOT`, `MARKET_TXN`), missions claimed (`MISSION_CLAIM_*`), systems visited per player + as a union, days elapsed, colony counters (Phase 24 wire), salvage events (`WORLD_DELTA(CONSUME)` ledger) — and the page's signature stat, **time flown together** (minutes both fleets shared a system or mutual sensor range; the host has both positions every frame, and it is the one stat single-player cannot have). *(**As built:** "together" is an id match on the containing location, hyperspace included, not sensor range, and it only accrues while the shared clock is running.)*
  - **One cheap optional message:** `SHIP_LOST` (name, class, system, day, cause) feeding a ship-loss ledger — losses as a story with named hulls, not a shame column — plus "days since the last hull loss" (Sea of Thieves' "days since last sinking").
  - **Dropped:** per-player kills/damage (needs new sync AND breeds friction), any composite score, any K/D-style ratio, accuracy-style rates.
  - **Presentation rules:** metrics as rows, players as columns in fixed order (host, then join order — never sorted by a stat), team column on the right; a team-first headline band, then 4–6 fixed superlative record cards (criterion printed on each card, ties print both names, suppressed below a trivial floor, at least one team-owned), then the per-player table in named sections, then a footer stating each stat's one-line attribution rule (every documented stat argument in the corpus traces to ambiguous attribution, not to the stat existing). **No per-row leader highlight** — the "green circle" is the one presentation element with a documented behavioral pathology (Darktide shipped without a scoreboard citing exactly it). A disconnected player's column renders "away", never blank. One screen; overflow goes to a second intel entry, not a scrollbar.
- **Polish pass** on Phase 20.6 surfaces: HUD widget styling/positioning option (`coop.hudCorner`), intel-entry layout, message colors — plus colored feed lines for every lobby event (joined / ready / unready / ready-reset reason / admitted / left). The feed is the most robust channel we have (it survives dialog teardown) and doubles as the session event log.

**Integration rule (corrected 2026-08-26 — supersedes the "register with the suspend logic" wording here and in 20.6):** dialogs are exclusive, so every coop dialog opens through the retry-until-true loop and closes via `dismiss()`. A real interaction dialog auto-suspends the `CoopCampaignInputBlocker` (the suspend flag is recomputed every frame from `ui.isShowingDialog()` — there is nothing to register). What still needs care: the lobby must yield to the interaction gate (never steal `showInteractionDialog` from an in-progress claim; the retry loop handles this by construction), and any future non-dialog input surface would need `CoopNetPump.isVanillaBlockingScreenOpen` extended, not the blocker touched.

**Agent prompt:**

```text
Implement Phase 21 from COOP_MP_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_V1.md. Build the multiplayer UX on official API surfaces only (no obf-UI access), following the phase's "Verified implementation facts" block and pinned Scope decisions exactly: in-campaign lobby as a plain InteractionDialogPlugin (option-panel gating per NewGameDialogPluginImpl, null target, retry-until-true open, no setOptionOnEscape, explicit three-step release incl. the hostPauseIntent clear), guest connecting dialog, three distinct desync dialogs (COOP-SEED / COOP-MODS / COOP-SESSION codes + [COOP-DOCTOR] log marker; requires adding a reason field to CoopSessionState.rejectHandshake), transient CoopSessionIntel stats page with the corrected stat set, and polish of the Phase 20.6 HUD/intel surfaces. Read-only display + session-start choreography only — no new sync protocols beyond the additive READY_STATE/SESSION_STATS(/SHIP_LOST) messages, no sim-affecting changes.
```

**Files:**

- Create `src/main/java/coop/ui/CoopLobbyDialog.java` (plain `InteractionDialogPlugin`: text-panel roster + option-panel ready/start gating; no custom panel unless the doctor-verdict block forces one)
- Create `src/main/java/coop/ui/CoopDesyncDialog.java` (three `showConfirmDialog` variants + the `[COOP-DOCTOR]` log-marker writer + reason→dialog mapping with the "Co-op session ended (<reason>)" fallback)
- Modify `src/main/java/coop/ui/CoopSessionIntel.java` (stats page; convert to the transient remove-before-save/recreate-on-load pattern if 20.6 didn't already build it that way)
- Modify `src/main/java/coop/net/CoopMessages.java` (`READY_STATE`, `SESSION_STATS`, optional `SHIP_LOST` — additive)
- Modify `src/main/java/coop/session/CoopSessionState.java` (lobby/ready states; `rejectHandshake` keeps its reason)
- Modify `src/main/java/coop/net/CoopNetPump.java` (all-ready gate on the existing hold; three-step release incl. `hostPauseIntent` clear; stats tally hooks)
- Unit tests (ready-state gating incl. revoke + reset-on-settings-change, admission serialization + abandonment cleanup, stats tally + attribution rules, reason→dialog mapping incl. fallback)

**Steps:**

- [x] Lobby/ready states in `CoopSessionState` + `READY_STATE` message + host-side all-ready gate that holds the connect-time pause until everyone readies (reuse the existing connect-pause hold — do not invent a second pause source); release = `dismiss()` + `setPaused(false)` + `hostPauseIntent` clear, after the cancellable ~3 s countdown. *(BUILT 2026-09-03, `5014011`. **As built:** the gate is `CoopSessionState.lobbyReleased`, cleared with the canonical session, and a new `isSessionPlayable()` = active ∧ released used at exactly two pump sites, the hold predicate and the shared-pause gate; every stream keeps flowing on `isGameplaySessionActive()` so the guest syncs while the lobby is open. The three-step release in the spec predates Phase 20 finding F2: the hold is owned, so on the release frame `consumeCoopHoldRelease()` seeds the host intent false and `syncHostSharedPause` unpauses in the same frame, with no new `setPaused` caller (pump test pins it). Roster = `coop.session.CoopLobbyRoster` (pure, host-authoritative): one admission in the handshake at a time, `dropPartial` before SNAPSHOT_APPLIED, `resetReady(reason)` exists but has no runtime caller because every host setting is launch-time. `READY_STATE` guest→host `{phase, ready}`, `LOBBY_STATUS` host→guest (roster, countdown remaining, released, elapsed) on change and at least once per second. SNAPSHOT_APPLIED = the guest holds a host `TIME_SNAPSHOT` (the only unconditional periodic host→guest stream; BASE_SET is change-driven and could wedge the gate). A resume inside the reconnect grace keeps the gate open; grace expiry clears it and the next handshake reopens the lobby. Safety valve: with no campaign UI at all the pump releases the gate itself rather than hold a world nobody can see.)*
- [x] `CoopLobbyDialog` host + guest variants (roster rows with state word + reason, ready/revoke options, Start with in-label blocking reason, confirm-guarded "Start anyway", doctor verdict text); opened via the deferral recipe on `onGameLoad`, ESC disabled, re-openable from pure session state for mid-session admit and reconnect. *(BUILT 2026-09-03. Plain `InteractionDialogPlugin` rendering a `CoopLobbyView` record, re-rendered on change only; "Start anyway" is an in-dialog two-step, never a nested dialog. The reconnect controller was generalised into `coop.ui.CoopDialogController` (any `InteractionDialogPlugin & CoopDismissableDialog`, retry-until-shown, two-frame deferral) and `CoopDialogArbiter` fixes precedence reconnect > desync > lobby > connecting.)*
- [x] "Connecting…" dialog for the guest between socket connect and lobby admit (phase list, live Cancel, cause-named failures). *(BUILT 2026-09-03, `CoopConnectingDialog`: stays up until SNAPSHOT_APPLIED then hands over to the lobby; 30 s timeout named as such; Cancel stops the connect loop and leaves the guest paused; steps aside when a desync dialog owns the failure.)*
- [x] Reason plumbing: `rejectHandshake` stores its reason; `CoopDesyncDialog` wired to seed-lock reject, resume reject (needs Phase 20), and handshake mismatch — three dialogs per the Scope spec, each with its `COOP-*` code and `[COOP-DOCTOR]` marker line. *(BUILT 2026-09-03. `CoopDesyncReason.classify(raw, Source)` parses the real producer strings (`CoopHandshakeDiff`, `CoopSeedSync`, the campaignId texts, `CoopReconnectCoordinator` reasons); the dialogs are interaction dialogs, not `showConfirmDialog`, because only those go through the retry-until-shown loop and carry highlighted paragraphs. Search string is `[COOP-DOCTOR] code=COOP-SEED` (the code is the marker's first field); UNMAPPED reuses `COOP-SESSION` so the greppable set stays at three. The correlation id is captured on the first line of every reject site, before the state clears it; lobby-stage rejects use the host-minted provisional lobby id. Deterministic rejects are terminal for the guest's launch (reuses the F4 wrong-password brake) so there is no reject→reconnect loop; the host rewinds to HOST_WAITING and its lobby returns after the dialog. No retry button anywhere: the only retryable reason (host still in grace) never stops the guest's connect loop, so a button would start nothing. "Ended by player" gets no dialog.)*
- [x] Host-tallied `SESSION_STATS` broadcast + intel stats page per the presentation rules (fixed column order, records cards, attribution footer, no leader highlight); optional `SHIP_LOST` ledger. *(BUILT 2026-09-03. `coop.stats.CoopSessionStats` (XStream-plain DTO under persistent-data key `coop.sessionStats`, aliases `coopStats`/`coopStatsPlayer`/`coopStatsLoss`, written by both roles so both coordinated saves carry the tallies), `CoopSessionStatsCodec` (flat JSON, lists in one delimited body), a second intel entry **"Coop Stats"** (`CoopSessionStatsIntel`, TIER_1, Refresh button, transient) with the pure `CoopSessionStatsView`. **Stat-set correction:** "team ships destroyed" is NOT on the wire (`BATTLE_RESULT` carries `destroyedFleetIds` only, own rosters deliberately not carried) → replaced by team fleets destroyed. Every tally sits behind the existing dedup (battle reconciler, txn replay guard, mission claim-holder read, world ledger + consumed, colony ledger). "Together" = same containing location (mutual sensor range is the unbuilt refinement); distance anchor resets on a location change. `SHIP_LOST` built on both roles: roster captured at battle start, diffed on the first 1 Hz sample after the post-battle screen closes. Known limits: the guest's best single trade is always 0 (`MARKET_TXN` carries `unitPrice=0`, a protocol change to fix), net worth = liquid credits for both. Broadcast once after the lobby release then every 30 s.)*
- [x] Polish pass (HUD corner option, colors, layout, lobby feed lines); update `docs/CONNECTIVITY.md` screenshots/walkthrough. *(BUILT 2026-09-03: `-Dcoop.hudCorner=TR|TL|BR|BL` (`CoopHudCorner`, anchor math unit-tested), HUD `in lobby` status, feed lines for join/ready/unready/drop/countdown/start; both coop intel entries converted to the transient remove-before-save/recreate-after pattern with a load-time sweep of instances persisted by older saves. Walkthrough: `docs/player/CONNECT.md` + `docs/CONNECTIVITY.md`.)*
- [x] Two-instance QA: lobby ready-up flow incl. revoke and "Start anyway", mid-handshake join, drop-during-handshake cleanup, every dialog vs. interaction-gate collision matrix; commit with `git commit -m "feat: multiplayer UX (lobby, desync dialogs, session stats)"`. *(SMOKE PASSED 2026-09-03 after seven fixes, `c5c50cb`, 2238 → 2275 tests; host and guest test clients on one machine, run together with Phase 28's smoke. **Passed as built:** ready-up including revoke and "Start anyway" (the confirm survived the re-render), countdown release, the "Coop Stats" page agreeing on both sides, a drop mid-countdown cancelling the countdown, grace expiry raising COOP-SESSION with `graceSeconds=60` and no retry button, a wrong-seed guest getting a terminal COOP-SEED with the HUD line and no retry loop, and a mod-version mismatch raising COOP-MODS on both sides under one `sessionId`. **Failed and fixed, each re-verified live on the rebuilt jar:** a relaunched guest was refused with "session in reconnect grace" until the window expired, and "wait longer" pushed that out by 5 minutes a press; the dropped guest's roster row outlived the wait, leaving a third row whose "Reconnecting" counter counted up from the drop; the guest's `SEED_LOCK_REJECT` was discarded by `stopReconnecting` before it reached the wire, so the host opened a 60 s grace for a guest that never passed the seed lock and then raised COOP-SESSION for what was COOP-SEED; the lobby, connecting and reconnect dialogs rebuilt their text panel every tick, which the player read as a full-panel flash once per second ("2 periodic flashes overlap, it's pretty much unreadable"); `-Dcoop.newGameSeed` with 19 nines crashed vanilla in `CampaignState.createUI` on long overflow; the docs named the user override file `coop_options.json` where the engine writes `coop_options.json.data`; and two desync copy defects (the host's campaign-mismatch page read as if the host had loaded the wrong save, and the session-ended advice told the host to reload with its own lobby already open). The rejoin model changed with the first fix: see the 2026-09-03 correction note under Phase 20.2.)*

**Acceptance:**

- A coop session starts through the lobby: both players see each other + connection verdict, Start arms only on all-ready (host auto-ready), the world stays paused until the host presses Start and the countdown completes — and the release actually unpauses (the `hostPauseIntent` latch is cleared, not just the hold stopped).
- The host can revoke the gate ("Start anyway") after a confirm; a guest can revoke ready; a host settings change resets guest ready with a feed line; ESC does not close the lobby.
- A guest connecting mid-session pauses the world and lands in the reopened lobby; a guest that drops mid-handshake leaves no partial state and no stuck row.
- Seed-lock reject / resume reject / handshake mismatch each produce their own dialog naming cause and remedy with a `COOP-*` code, and write one `[COOP-DOCTOR]` line whose `sessionId` matches across both players' logs; an unmapped reason still produces a dialog, never a silent end.
- Session stats are visible on both clients, survive save/load of the coordinated saves (tallies persist; the intel entry itself is transient and recreated), and a solo load of a coop save shows no coop intel entry.
- No dialog deadlocks: lobby/desync dialogs never trap a player with the interaction gate or input blocker (collision matrix passes).
- Solo (non-coop) launches show none of this UI and remain byte-for-byte vanilla.

## Phase 22: Co-op Piloted Battles + Tactical-Map Observer (post-V1)

> **Goal:** both players fighting in the *same* battle — the long-deferred "joint combat" — via the architecture identified 2026-06-10: **don't replicate the battle, replicate the pilot.** One combat engine (the host's) runs a real battle containing both players' ships; the guest pilots one of its *own* ships by streaming inputs over the Phase 20 UDP link into a keyboard-driven `ShipAIPlugin`. No PvP — both players are always on the player side against NPCs. **Ordering:** post-V1, after Phase 21; depends on Phase 14 (mirror battles, `BATTLE_RESULT`) and Phase 20 (sequenced UDP). Milestone 0 (the tactical-map observer) is independently valuable, lands first, and is the committed fallback if piloted-co-op latency disappoints.

**Verified facts (2026-06-10, from the MIT-licensed "Cooperative Combat" mod by Nick XR — clone at `tmp_ff_analysis\coopcombat_repo`, v3.10.0, maintained for 0.98a-RC7):**

- **Second-pilot control through official API is proven and maintained:** `CoOpShipAI implements ShipAIPlugin`, assigned with `ship.setShipAI(...)`, polls a keyboard each frame and drives the ship via `ship.giveCommand(...)` / `ship.useSystem()` / `ship.getMouseTarget()`. Up to 3 simultaneous pilots on one engine. MIT license — vendoring with attribution is allowed.
- **Every no-mouse problem is already solved** in that codebase: the active weapon group is forced to aim at `ship.getFacing()` each frame; `NEXT_TARGET`/`PREV_TARGET` cycling drives `setShipTarget`; omni shields are aimed by a helper AI at the nearest threat via `getMouseTarget()`; ship systems target the selected target; per-pilot reticle/flux/cooldown overlays draw in `renderInWorldCoords`.
- **Engine gotchas are documented there and must be preserved:** only one `TOGGLE_AUTOFIRE` command lands per frame (queue them); capture each ship's default autofire state and restore it before `resetDefaultAI()`; `giveCommand` is dead while paused; the AI is retrieved via the `Ship.ShipAIWrapper` cast.
- **The input seam is one line:** `KeyToCommand.shouldGiveCommand()` wraps `Keyboard.isKeyDown(keyIndex)`. Replacing that read with a network-fed virtual key state converts the whole design from shared-keyboard to networked input.
- **What the repo does NOT change:** it contains zero networking (verified by search) and never replicates combat state — one machine simulates everything. The Phase 14 verdict stands: rendered remote combat remains INFEASIBLE, so the guest cannot watch the host's battle through our code.

**Architecture:**

- **One engine, host-side.** The joint battle runs only on the host. The guest's fleet fights as an allied player-side participant via its Phase 14 mirror joining the `BattleAPI` (the engine supports allied fleets in player battles natively). For sessions with this phase enabled, this replaces Phase 14's both-players-in-one-battle stopgap (host pilots everything, guest spectates).
- **Combat-grade fleet manifest — the real new sync work.** The host-side mirror must carry battle-grade truth for the guest fleet: variants (hull + s-mods + weapon groups), officers + skills, CR, hull/armor fractions. `FLEET_MANIFEST` snapshot at battle start (TCP); results return through the existing `BATTLE_RESULT` path and apply to the guest's authoritative fleet.
- **Networked virtual keyboard.** The guest polls its own LWJGL keyboard and streams an action-state record (the 18 pilot actions: held-state bitmap + per-action press counters so toggle commands survive packet loss) over the Phase 20 sequenced UDP channel, on-change up to ~30 Hz, epoch latest-wins. The host feeds it into the vendored pilot AI through a `KeyToCommand`-shaped adapter whose key-read is the virtual state instead of `Keyboard.isKeyDown`. Payload ≤ ~16 B + envelope. This is the one sanctioned UDP combat stream — it carries *inputs*, not state; Phase 14's "no UDP in combat" rule was about state replication and stands.
- **The guest's eyes are out-of-band, and the docs must say so plainly:** piloted co-op requires a video stream of the host's screen (Parsec / Discord / Steam Remote Play). The control scheme survives streaming because it is keyboard-only by design — no mouse aiming to break. Expected control loop = input RTT + stream latency ≈ 100–250 ms: playable in destroyers and up, rough in frigates. Set expectations in the docs and verify in QA at shaped latency.
- **Milestone 0 — tactical-map observer (build first).** *(2026-08-19: Phase 14's `CoopBattleStatusPanel` was deleted — the spectator is banners now — so this milestone builds the panel from scratch instead of upgrading one. The `BATTLE_STATUS` capture/stream/codec/kill-feed it needs were deliberately kept alive and are still fed at 2.5 Hz.)* Build a drawn tactical map: extend `BATTLE_STATUS` with optional position/facing fields (5–10 Hz, still TCP), render ship blips + hull/flux bars with our own GL inside the custom panel (the vendored repo's `PlayerShip`/`TargetedShip`/flux-bar rendering is the cookbook; `CustomUIPanelPlugin.render` is the official hook). No projectiles — an observer doesn't need them. This is also the no-video-stream observer experience during piloted battles.
- **Possession rule:** each player may only possess ships their own fleet contributed (manifest-tagged); the host pilots normally with the mouse, the guest uses the keyboard scheme. Ship-select arbitration reuses the repo's in-use-ship list, keyed by owner.

**Integration rule:** the vendored pilot AI and the tactical panel follow the same suspend/exclusivity discipline as every coop dialog (`CoopCampaignInputBlocker` registration; the tactical panel is an `InteractionDialogPlugin` like the status panel it replaces).

**Agent prompt:**

```text
Implement Phase 22 from COOP_MP_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_V1.md (post-V1; requires Phases 14 and 20 complete). Build milestone 0 first (tactical-map observer upgrade of the battle-status panel), then the spikes, then piloted co-op: one battle on the host engine containing both players' ships; the guest pilots its own ship via a networked virtual keyboard (KEY_STATE over sequenced UDP, loss-safe press counters) feeding a vendored, MIT-attributed CoOpShipAI whose keyboard read is replaced by the virtual state. The guest's view is an out-of-band video stream — document that requirement; never attempt combat-state replication (verified infeasible). No PvP: both players always fight on the player side, and each player may only possess their own ships.
```

**Files:**

- Create `src/main/java/coop/combat/CoopRemotePilotAI.java` (vendored `CoOpShipAI` + helpers, MIT attribution header, virtual-key adapter seam)
- Create `src/main/java/coop/combat/CoopVirtualKeyboard.java` (guest-side capture + `KEY_STATE` codec + host-side adapter)
- Create `src/main/java/coop/combat/CoopFleetManifest.java` (combat-grade guest-fleet snapshot + apply-to-mirror)
- Create `src/main/java/coop/combat/CoopTacticalMapPanel.java` (milestone 0: drawn tactical-map observer)
- Create `src/main/java/coop/rewards/CoopRewardSplitter.java` (promoted from the v1 exclusion note — joint battles need a spoils rule)
- Modify `src/main/java/coop/net/CoopMessages.java` (`FLEET_MANIFEST` TCP, `KEY_STATE` UDP, additive `BATTLE_STATUS` position/facing fields)
- Modify `src/main/java/coop/combat/CoopBattleBridge.java` + **create** `CoopBattleStatusPanel.java` (joint-battle lifecycle, tactical panel — *2026-08-19: the Phase 14 panel of that name was deleted, so this is a new file again*)
- Unit tests (`KEY_STATE` codec + press-counter loss recovery, manifest round-trip, possession arbitration, reward split)

**Steps:**

- [ ] **Milestone 0:** additive `BATTLE_STATUS` position/facing fields + `CoopTacticalMapPanel` drawing blips/bars; ships as the observer view for all Phase 14 battles. Independently shippable.
- [ ] **Spike A (feasibility gate):** the guest's mirror fleet joins a host battle as a player-side ally via `BattleAPI` and deploys, AI-piloted — no remote pilot yet. If allied-join fights back, this phase stops at milestone 0.
- [ ] **Spike B:** vendored pilot AI drives a ship from a scripted/replayed virtual key feed on loopback (autofire queue, toggle debounce, shield AI all working without a physical keyboard).
- [ ] **Spike C (latency gate):** pilot over shaped 100/200 ms RTT + 2 % loss with a real video stream; record the verdict per hull size in the docs.
- [ ] `FLEET_MANIFEST` snapshot at battle start; mirror upgraded to battle-grade fidelity; results apply back via `BATTLE_RESULT`.
- [ ] `KEY_STATE` channel (bitmap + press counters, epoch latest-wins) + host adapter replacing `Keyboard.isKeyDown`.
- [ ] Possession arbitration (own ships only, one pilot per ship) + guest HUD overlays (reticle/flux/cooldown from the vendored renderers).
- [ ] Reward split: each player keeps own-hull damage/CR consequences; loot/credits split via `CoopRewardSplitter` (default 50/50, `coop.lootSplit` configurable on the Phase 28 options registry).
- [ ] Docs: video-stream setup guide (Parsec/Discord/Remote Play), latency expectations, keybind setup for the guest scheme.
- [ ] Two-instance QA at shaped WAN settings; commit with `git commit -m "feat: co-op piloted battles + tactical-map observer"`.

**Acceptance:**

- The observer view for any Phase 14 battle is a drawn tactical map (positions, facings, hull/flux) at 5–10 Hz, not a text list; it degrades to the text panel if position fields are absent (old peer).
- With the phase enabled, a host-engine battle contains both players' ships; the guest possesses and pilots one of its own ships end-to-end (movement, weapons, shields, system, venting, target cycling) via `KEY_STATE` over UDP.
- Toggle commands are loss-safe: 2 % packet loss produces no stuck or double-fired toggles (press counters verified under shaped loss).
- The guest's fleet takes real consequences: hull damage, CR loss, and losses from the joint battle apply to the guest's authoritative fleet; spoils split per the configured rule.
- No PvP: possession of the other player's ships is impossible; both players are always on the same side.
- With the phase disabled (or spike A failed), Phase 14 behavior is unchanged except the upgraded tactical-map observer.

## Phase 23: Public Release Packaging (V1 since 2026-09-03)

> **Pulled into V1, 2026-09-03 (user decision, while Phase 21 was in build).** Order: after Phase 28 (whose milestone 1 is this phase's settings-file bullet), before the Phase 19 sign-off. Scope unchanged.

> **Decided 2026-06-10 ("public after soak"):** V1 ships privately first (host + one friend); a public forum/Nexus release is the goal after the private soak proves stability. This phase turns the developer-grade deliverable into a releasable mod. It runs **after Phase 21** (the lobby/desync dialogs are part of what makes the mod presentable) and does **not** require Phase 22.

**Scope:**

- **Player-facing docs:** an install guide — including the vmparams classpath-prepend for `coop-forks.jar`, which is unusual for a mod and needs careful copy-pasteable instructions; a connect guide (distilled `CONNECTIVITY.md`: port-forward/UPnP/VPN walkthroughs, connection-doctor interpretation); a known-limitations page (distilled `starsector-runtime-limitations.md`: accepted divergences, the guest-save policy, the solo own-fleet combat model). Written for players, not developers.
- **Launch UX:** friendlier session start — config read from a data/config file (via the sanctioned `SettingsAPI` surfaces) instead of raw `-D` properties where feasible — **this settings-file surface is now owned by Phase 28 milestone 1** (typed options registry + `saves/common` overrides), which should land with this phase; launch scripts hardened for machines that are not the dev box (path detection, no hardcoded `K:\`). The title-screen limit is permanent (no API — Phase 21 facts), so the in-campaign lobby remains the first interactive surface.
- **Release hygiene:** version-string discipline (`mod_info.json` + the handshake build hash must agree), a player-facing CHANGELOG, license choice for the mod, MIT attribution for vendored CoOpCombat code if Phase 22 shipped, and listing text with an honest feature/limitation summary.
- **Support posture:** the Phase 21 desync/reject dialogs name cause + remedy; document how users report issues (which connection-doctor/log lines to paste) so the doctor log is the support tool it was built to be.

**Acceptance:**

- A stranger with two matching Starsector installs can download the mod, follow the docs, and reach a working session without reading developer docs; the only manual system-level step is the documented vmparams edit.
- The public listing's claims match what the mod actually does, including the limitations.

- [x] Harden launch scripts for non-dev machines (path detection, no hardcoded `K:\`). *(2026-09-03: shared `scripts/coop-paths.ps1` derives mod root, install root and test-client root from the script location, with parameters to override and a loud failure naming the parameter; every switch kept; dry runs produced byte-identical targets on the dev box.)*

CoOpCombat attribution waits for Phase 22. Still open: the forum-thread URL for the listing. ## Phase 24: Shared-Faction Colonies, Industries, and Raids

> **Rescoped into V1 (2026-06-10, user decision — originally sketched post-V1 the same day).** Slots after 12c in the Implementation Order; milestone 1 (raids) needs no colony-lifecycle work and can start any time after 12c lands.
>
> **Feasibility assessed 2026-06-10 (API-verified): FEASIBLE as a shared player faction.** Both players manage the *same* colonies under the one engine player faction. Everything rides machinery V1 already builds — host-authoritative markets (12/12c), the world-skeleton delta channel (DECIV et al.), the interaction gate (10), outcome-delta capture (15's pattern), rep deltas (8), `CoopRewardSplitter` (created **by this phase** in `coop.rewards` for the income split; Phase 22 extends it post-V1). The genuinely new work is one thing: **market lifecycle replication** (a colony is a market that exists on only one engine until we create it on the other). Separate per-player factions are the stretch goal — assessed and **rejected**, see below. Prereqs: 12c (full market capture), 13 (suppressor — `PlayerRelatedPirateBaseManager` is already on its list), 16 (coordinated saves must capture colony state, which they do for free — it lives in the host save).

**Engine facts (verified against the 0.98a API sources, 2026-06-10):**

- `MarketAPI` exposes the full colony-management mutation surface as public API: `addIndustry(id)` / `removeIndustry(id, mode, forUpgrade)`, `getConstructionQueue()`, `setPlayerOwned(boolean)`, conditions, submarkets (`MarketAPI.java:278-411`). Building a market from scratch on the other engine is plain API — `Misc.java:6524-6539` even carries the recipe in commented-out form.
- `PlayerColonizationListener.reportPlayerColonizedPlanet(PlanetAPI)` fires on colonization — a clean capture hook on whichever engine the colonizing player runs.
- **Every player hostile act against a colony has a vanilla outcome listener**: `ColonyPlayerHostileActListener` provides `reportRaidForValuablesFinishedBeforeCargoShown(..., CargoAPI loot)`, `reportRaidToDisruptFinished(..., Industry)`, `reportTacticalBombardmentFinished`, `reportSaturationBombardmentFinished`; plus `GroundRaidObjectivesListener.reportRaidObjectivesAchieved(RaidResultData, ...)`. Raid resolution itself is open-source rules code (`MarketCMD`), so semantics are inspectable.
- `EconomyTickListener.reportEconomyMonthEnd()` is the colony-income capture point.
- NPC threats against player colonies (expeditions, pirate raids, Pather cells) are host-simulated already under Phase 13's suppressor model; their fleets reach the guest via Phase 9 replication.

**The model — one faction, two governors:**

1. **Colony lifecycle replication (the new core).** Colonization is a player action resolved locally by whoever performs it (vanilla dialog, marines, rolls — consistent with "replicate outcomes, not RNG"); the colonization listener fires and ships a `COLONY_FOUNDED` delta (planet id, market seed data: size, conditions, starting industries, submarkets). The host canonicalizes and rebroadcasts; the receiving engine builds the same market via the public API recipe and `setPlayerOwned(true)`. Abandonment ships the inverse delta. After creation, the colony is just another market — Phase 12/12c snapshot-on-open + transaction deltas already govern its contents.
2. **Colony management (industries).** The guest opens the mirrored player-owned market and the engine gives it the full vanilla colony UI locally — no custom UI needed. Mutations (industry add/remove/upgrade, construction queue, AI cores, improvements, free port, governor settings) are captured by **diff-on-close** (`ColonyInteractionListener.reportPlayerClosedMarket`: compare against the open-time snapshot) → `COLONY_MGMT` delta → host applies to the canonical market → rebroadcast. **Concurrency is already solved:** the Phase 10 interaction gate arbitrates one-player-per-entity dialogs, so two players cannot be inside the same colony screen at once — no concurrent-edit conflicts by construction. *(**False as written, corrected 2026-09-04:** the gate keys on the entity a dialog opened, and the colony screen reached from the command tab opens no dialog, so both players could edit the same colony and the later absolute report silently dropped the other's edit. The `OUTPOSTS` tab now claims the synthetic id `coop:colony-management` through the same gate; the second player in is bounced to the INTEL tab. No engine call says which colony that tab is showing, so the claim covers the whole editor.)*
3. **Income and spoils.** Monthly colony income lands on the host engine (`reportEconomyMonthEnd`). Split via `CoopRewardSplitter` policy (**50/50, decided 2026-06-10** — the class is created by this phase; same `coop.lootSplit` family Phase 22 extends later); the guest gets a monthly report line (feed message or session intel). Colony storage/local-resources submarkets are shared *claimable* content per the Phase 12c boundary — both players see and use the same storage.
4. **Player raids and bombardments on NPC colonies.** The acting player resolves the raid locally against the snapshot-fresh mirrored market (marine choices, target picks, rolls — all local); the hostile-act listeners capture the complete outcome → `RAID_RESULT` delta (commodities removed, industry + disruption days, stability hit, rep deltas via the existing `REP_DELTA` channel). Loot is per-player like salvage. Saturation-bombardment deciv flows through the existing `DECIV` skeleton delta unchanged. Host applies to the canonical market and rebroadcasts. *(**True only for a host bombardment; corrected 2026-09-04 (`ac837b9`):** the `DECIV` comes from the host's own `ColonyDecivListener`, which a guest bombardment never fires. The guest sent `RAID_RESULT` alone and the applier assumed a `DECIV` had already arrived, so the host kept the colony while the guest deleted it and the next reconnect was rejected at the fingerprint. `handleRaidResult` now calls the extracted `CoopCampaignReplicator.decivilizeMarket(marketId, fullDestroy, source)` itself when `outcome.decivilized()`, outside the replay guard so the host's listener broadcasts the normal `DECIV` onward.)*
5. **NPC threats against the players' colonies.** Expeditions/raids targeting player colonies simulate host-side only (suppressor holds guest-side; `PlayerRelatedPirateBaseManager` suppression is already specced in Phase 13). Their fleets replicate via Phase 9; defense battles ride Phase 14 (and Phase 22 if built). Gap to close here: the *warning intel* (expedition countdown) is host-local — **decided 2026-06-10: mirror it as a coop-owned intel item** (`CoopExpeditionWarningIntel` + `EXPEDITION_WARNING`, see the settled decision points below) so the guest gets a real countdown entry, not just a feed line.

**Decision points — SETTLED (user decisions, 2026-06-10 review pass):**

- **Income split: 50/50, hardwired for V1.** Each monthly tick splits evenly; the guest receives the credit delta plus a monthly report line. `coop.incomeSplit` on the Phase 28 options registry makes it configurable post-V1.
- **Guest colonization: trusted, no host consent.** The guest colonizes like any other action; the host just canonicalizes the `COLONY_FOUNDED` delta. No confirm dialog in V1 (avoids the blocking-dialog suspend-logic trap). `coop.guestColonizationConsent` on the Phase 28 registry adds the toggle post-V1, default trusted.
- **Expedition warning: mirrored intel item** (the richer option, chosen over a feed line). Implemented as a **coop-owned** intel entry — `coop.colony.CoopExpeditionWarningIntel`, a custom `IntelInfoPlugin` (the verified surface also used by 20.6's `CoopSessionIntel`) — fed by an `EXPEDITION_WARNING` TCP message (faction, kind, target market id, ETA days, status), updated on change, removed on resolution. This mirrors the warning *data* into a coop intel item; it does **not** replicate the vanilla intel object (no generic intel-replication machinery).

**Stretch goal — separate per-player factions: assessed INFEASIBLE-IN-PRACTICE, rejected (2026-06-10).** The engine hardwires exactly one player faction: colony management UI, construction queues, income, production, storage access, commissions, and the hostility matrix all key off `Factions.PLAYER` / `market.isPlayerOwned()`. A guest-owned separate faction would have to exist as a custom *NPC* faction on the host engine — which gets **no management UI at all** (that entire surface would be custom-built), plus a permanent cross-engine identity/rep/ownership mapping layer (guest-engine player faction ↔ host-engine `coop_guest` faction) threaded through every rep, economy, targeting, and intel system. That mapping layer alone is larger than the rest of this phase combined, and every vanilla update would stress it — squarely against the stability rubric. The shared-faction model also matches the co-op fiction (one faction, two leaders). Do not relitigate without a new engine surface.

**Files:**

- Create `src/main/java/coop/colony/CoopColonySync.java` (lifecycle deltas + management diff-on-close)
- Create `src/main/java/coop/colony/CoopRaidOutcomeSync.java` (hostile-act listener capture + apply)
- Create `src/main/java/coop/colony/CoopExpeditionWarningIntel.java` (coop-owned mirrored expedition-warning intel entry — decided 2026-06-10)
- Modify `src/main/java/coop/net/CoopMessages.java` (`COLONY_FOUNDED`, `COLONY_ABANDONED`, `COLONY_MGMT`, `RAID_RESULT`, `EXPEDITION_WARNING` — reliable TCP)
- Create `src/main/java/coop/rewards/CoopRewardSplitter.java` (V1 scope: colony-income split policy, default 50/50 — **this phase creates the class**; Phase 22 (post-V1) later extends it for joint-combat spoils. Package is `coop.rewards`, matching the Source Layout — an earlier draft said `coop/campaign`, which was wrong)
- Unit tests (delta codecs, market-build recipe round-trip, management diff correctness, income-split math, expedition-warning intel lifecycle)

**Steps (milestone order — each independently shippable):**

- [x] **Milestone 1 — raids first** (no lifecycle work needed: NPC markets exist on both engines already): hostile-act listener capture → `RAID_RESULT` → host apply + rebroadcast; two-instance QA of a guest raid and a host raid against the same NPC colony. *(Built 2026-08-25: `coop.colony.CoopRaidOutcomeSync` — codec + `Ledger` + `HostileActCapture` + apply — plus `RAID_RESULT` (reliable TCP, one escaped `outcome` blob) and the replicator wiring. **Payload:** header line `H|outcomeId|kind|marketId|actingPlayerId|marketSize|unrestPenalty|unrestReason|pollutionAdded|solarArrayRemoved|decivilized`, then one `I|industryId|disruptedDays|aiCoreId|specialItemId|specialItemData` per touched industry and one `C|commodityId|units` per commodity deficit; `CoopDelimited`-escaped, `CoopBaseRecord.encodeSet` convention. **Values are absolute but scoped**: absolute reads taken off the live market after vanilla finished (echo-safe, no compounding), yet only for the industries the act actually touched — the touched set comes from `TempData.bombardmentTargets` / the assigned `objectives`' sources, because a full absolute sweep shipped from the guest would overwrite host-side disruption its mirror had gone stale on. **Unrest convergence:** no delta on the wire. `RecentUnrest.add` is additive and files the tooltip reason, `setPenalty` is an absolute write with none (`RecentUnrest.java:54-58`, `:125-127`), so the applier derives the delta from *its own* current penalty, uses `add` for it, and falls through to the absolute write when the mirror drifted high; re-applying computes a zero delta and rewrites the same absolute. A zero-penalty outcome never touches `RecentUnrest.get`, which would create the condition just to say there is none. **SAT_BOMB/DECIV residue:** when the act decivilized, the outcome applies the `RECENTLY_BOMBARDED` market-memory flag and *nothing else* — `DecivTracker.decivilize` strips industries, people, commodities and every `isDecivRemove` condition (`DecivTracker.java:206-239`), and it runs *before* the listener fires (`MarketCMD.java:2646` vs `:2652`) so the DECIV delta lands first; market memory is the one thing it does not touch. Non-destroying saturation bombardment ships absolute size and steps down through `CoreImmigrationPluginImpl.reduceMarketSize` (`:169-184`), never up. **Dedup key** = `actingPlayerId + ":" + monotonic counter`, session-scoped, cleared in `dispose`; capture short-circuits on `replayGuard.isReplaying()` and the per-`TempData`-identity guard collapses vanilla's per-industry disrupt report (`MarketCMD.java:1903-1906`) into one outcome. **Corrections to the spec's assumptions:** "commodities removed" is wrong — a commodity raid removes nothing, it hangs a temporary negative flat mod on the availability stat (`CommodityGroundRaidObjectivePluginImpl.java:214-221`), invisible to the Phase 12 market channel, so it rides here with a deterministic source id instead of vanilla's `Misc.genUID()`. Raids also strip AI cores and special items from industries and can destroy a solar array (`AICoreGroundRaidObjectivePluginImpl.java:91-95`, `SpecialItemRaidObjectivePluginImpl.java:106-112`, `SolarArrayGroundRaidObjectivePluginImpl.java:34`) — all carried. Conditions are one-directional presence assertions, not an absolute set: we only observed POLLUTION and SOLAR_ARRAY change, so asserting the whole set would be a claim we cannot back. The `$raid_cooldown` sector-memory key is deliberately **not** replicated — it is the acting fleet's own cooldown (`MarketCMD.java:1516-1518`), and mirroring it would lock the other player out of a raid they never made. 27 new tests (21 `CoopRaidOutcomeSyncTest`, 6 `CoopRaidReplicatorTest`), suite 959 → 986 green. Test note: proxying `Industry` forces `MarketCMD.RaidDangerLevel`'s static init, which needs a stubbed `SettingsAPI` or the proxy class itself fails to initialize.)*
    - [x] Residue: two-instance QA of a guest raid and a host raid against the same NPC colony (manual, both clients). *(PASSED 2026-09-01 — see the live-smoke entry at the end of this step list.)*
- [x] **Milestone 2 — colony lifecycle:** `COLONY_FOUNDED`/`COLONY_ABANDONED` deltas + market-build recipe; QA: guest colonizes, host sees the colony, both reopen after save/load. *(Built 2026-08-25: `coop.colony.CoopColonySync` — codec + `Ledger` + `ColonizationCapture` + apply — plus `COLONY_FOUNDED`/`COLONY_ABANDONED` (reliable TCP, one escaped `colony` blob each) and the replicator wiring. **Payload:** header line `H|eventId|kind|planetId|marketId|actingPlayerId|name|factionId|size|freePort|surveyLevel|storageUnlocked`, then one `C|conditionId|surveyed` per condition, one `I|industryId`, one `S|submarketSpecId`; `CoopDelimited`-escaped, `CoopBaseRecord.encodeSet` convention. **No id mapping needed, confirmed:** colonization does not create a market — every planet already carries a gen-time planet-condition market created as `createMarket("market_" + planet.getId(), …)` (`PlanetConditionGenerator.java:134`, `Misc.java:3581`) and colonizing promotes that same object, so seed-locked worldgen gives both engines the same id. **But that market is not in the economy** — nothing in the gen path calls `addMarket`, and `CoreLifecyclePluginImpl.java:1122` says so out loud — so `economy.getMarket(id)` answers only for a live colony and the applier resolves through `sector.getEntityById(planetId).getMarket()` first, economy second. Both ids ride the wire anyway. **Deferred founding capture (deviation from the M1 shape):** `reportPlayerColonizedPlanet` hands over only a `PlanetAPI` and is fired from the closed campaign code, so its position in the construction sequence is unknowable; the listener therefore only marks the planet pending and the snapshot is taken from the replicator's next frame (`tickColonyLifecycle`, bounded at 600 drains ≈ 10 s, then logged and dropped). Abandonment is captured inline because vanilla fires *that* half after its own teardown has already run (`AbandonMarketPluginImpl.java:121-123` — the same reason `PlaythroughLog` always logs "Abandoned size 1", an upstream bug). **Abandon = one vanilla call:** `DecivTracker.removeColony(market, false)`, exactly what `abandonConfirmed` calls, which clears the admin, neutralizes connected entities, sets `planetConditionMarketOnly`, drops faction to neutral, clears comm directory/people/commodities, removes every `isDecivRemove` condition (the population condition included), every industry and every submarket (storage contents with them — vanilla's own prompt warns about it), sets `$wasCivilized`, forces size 1 with the population weight renormalized, `setPlayerOwned(false)` and removes the market from the economy (`DecivTracker.java:291-360`). The evacuation cost and shutdown refund deliberately do not replicate: the abandoning player's own wallet, same rule as loot. **Recipe correction:** the commented `Misc.createColonyStatic` (`Misc.java:6510-6543`) is a sketch, not a working sequence — it calls `setSize(3)` *before* `setPlanetConditionMarketOnly(false)`, and while that flag is set the engine's market reports size 1 and ignores `setSize` outright (`PlanetConditionMarket.getSize` returns a constant, `setSize` is an empty method). The applier follows the live promotion in `PK_CMD.convertSentinelToColony` instead: clear the flag, then conditions, then size + `reapplyConditions`, then industries, submarkets, survey level, `addMarket` if not already in the economy, primary-entity faction, `setPlayerOwned(true)` + free port + `reapplyIndustries`, then the storage unlock. **Conditions are an absolute set** (add missing, drop extra, align surveyed flags), not one-directional presence assertions — the only shape that expresses colonization's `DECIVILIZED` → `DECIVILIZED_SUBPOP` swap, and safe because both engines start from the same seed-locked planet conditions. **Storage unlock is write-only:** `StoragePlugin.playerPaidToUnlock` is private with a setter and no getter (`StoragePlugin.java:19`, `:90-92`), so it cannot be read back; founding always pays it off, so the payload carries "the colony has a storage submarket" and the applier makes the same unconditional call vanilla's recipe does. **Ledger semantics differ from M1 on purpose:** a colony is a value that oscillates (found → abandon → re-found on the same planet), so applied event ids kill the echo while a per-planet latest-kind map makes it latest-wins — every genuine transition applies once, and a stale FOUNDED arriving at an already-founded planet is a no-op. Dedup key = `actingPlayerId + ":" + monotonic counter`, session-scoped, cleared in `dispose`; capture short-circuits on `replayGuard.isReplaying()`. **Fingerprint verdict — no exclusion, verified:** both sides recompute the seed-lock fingerprint live from their own loaded save at every seed lock (`CoopNetPump.maybeSendSeedLockRequest` falls through to the live supplier because a stored `SeedData` carries no fingerprint), and on the supported resume path both saves carry the same replicated colony, so a colony is one added entry identical on both sides; before colonization there is no entry at all because a planet-condition market is never in `getMarketsCopy()`, and abandonment removes the entry symmetrically rather than leaving a size-1 ghost. The only fingerprint input a colony moves on its own is `marketSize`, and colony growth is **fully deterministic** — deep-read of `CoreImmigrationPluginImpl` and every `MarketImmigrationModifier` found zero RNG, and `ImmigrationTask` calls `advance(30f/economyIterPerMonth=10)` so `f` is always exactly `0.1` — making it a state-mirroring risk of the same class every market already carries, not an asymmetric-by-construction input like the hidden dynamic bases `includeMarket()` drops. Divergence hazards worth remembering for M3, none of them fingerprint-specific: `CoreImmigrationPluginImpl`'s once-ever 100-iteration burn-in when `incoming` is null (`:65-74`), `getAllImmigrationModifiers()`'s insertion-ordered float sum, and equal economy *iteration counts* rather than equal elapsed days. **Also landed here:** the Phase 13 base-capture switch from manager poll to intel-manager scan (see that phase's note). 33 new tests (22 `CoopColonySyncTest`, 6 `CoopColonyReplicatorTest`, 4 `CoopBaseAuthorityTest`, 1 `CoopSectorFingerprintTest`), suite 986 → 1019 green. M3 seam: `CoopColonySync` extends by adding fields to the industry record line or new record tags — both engines run the same build, so there is no wire-compat window. **Correction (2026-08-25, found live in a two-instance session):** the payload above shipped without the construction queue because this note assumed a just-founded colony has none — wrong. Vanilla colonization **auto-queues a `SPACEPORT`** the player never orders, so a founding is one industry (population) *plus* a one-entry queue; the host's capture logged `industries=1`, the mirror was built queue-empty, and it only converged if someone later opened and closed the colony screen so M3's diff-on-close shipped the queue. `COLONY_FOUNDED` now carries it as `Q|industryId|cost` lines after the `S` lines, reusing M3's `CoopColonyManagement.QueueItem` record, its codec and its reconciler verbatim (`QUEUE_TAG`, `captureQueue(market)` extracted from M3's `capture`, and `applyQueue(market, List<QueueItem>)` lifted off `State` — all package-visible) rather than minting a second format for the same thing. The queue is read in the same pass as the industries off the same deferred-drain snapshot, so no capture-timing change was needed; the apply is replace-only-if-different, so a re-applied founding never appends a duplicate and a payload with zero `Q` lines is a legitimately empty queue. 5 new tests (`CoopColonySyncTest`), suite 1136 → 1141 green. **Correction 2026-08-25 (live two-instance session):** a live founding was reported as the guest visibly *constructing* the “Population & Infrastructure” the host had finished at founding. **The founding applier was not the cause and the report was read back to its source.** The engine's own `Market.addIndustry` instantiates the industry plugin, appends it and calls `apply()` — there is no `startBuilding` anywhere on that path — and the session's own log line reads `Coop built mirrored colony market_penelope2 … industries=1`, i.e. exactly the one finished population industry the host had. What renders as a build bar is **vanilla's own display**: `PopulationAndInfrastructure` overrides `isBuilding()`/`isUpgrading()`/`getBuildOrUpgradeProgress()` to show a colony's growth toward its maximum size (`PopulationAndInfrastructure.java:606-617`), labelled “total growth: N%”, and the host shows the identical bar. The *completion notification* was real but belonged to M3, which took that override at face value and called `finishBuildingOrUpgrading()` on a non-build — firing vanilla's “construction completed” message and popping the construction queue a step early (`BaseIndustry.buildingFinished`); fixed in the M3 correction below. The applier gained one defensive step all the same: after `addIndustry`, an I-line industry that reads as *genuinely* building (via the new `CoopColonyManagement.liveBuildState`, never `isBuilding()`) is finished, which covers the one case `addIndustry` cannot — the industry was already present here and mid-build from a partially applied earlier state. I-lines end finished, Q-lines stay queued. 3 new tests (`CoopColonySyncTest`).)*
    - [x] Residue *(PASSED 2026-09-01 — see the live-smoke entry at the end of this step list)*: two-instance QA of a guest colonization and a host colonization (manual, both clients) — guest colonizes, host sees the colony with matching size/conditions/industries, both survive the coordinated save/load cycle, then abandon and confirm the planet returns to an uncolonized planet-condition market on both. *(Do not hunt the map for a target: `ss_dump(instance: "guest", what: "colonizable")` names the nearest uncolonized planets with their hazard, conditions and survey level, and `teleport` + `surveyset` + `give` set the run up from there. `ss_dump(what: "landmarks")` finds the hypershunts, cryosleepers, gates and stable locations to site one near.)*
- [x] **Milestone 3 — management + income:** diff-on-close `COLONY_MGMT` deltas, interaction-gate the colony screen, income split (50/50, via the new `coop.rewards.CoopRewardSplitter`) + monthly report line; **expedition-warning mirror:** host captures expedition/raid intel against player colonies via an intel-manager scan (the Phase 13 capture pattern) → `EXPEDITION_WARNING` TCP → guest creates/updates/removes its `CoopExpeditionWarningIntel` entry. QA: guest builds an industry, host sees it under construction; an incoming expedition shows the warning intel with countdown on the guest. *(Built 2026-08-25: `coop.colony.CoopColonyManagement` (codec + `Ledger` + `Diff` + apply), `coop.colony.CoopColonyIncome` (report reader + `MonthEndCapture` + wallet write), `coop.rewards.CoopRewardSplitter`, `coop.colony.CoopExpeditionWarning` (record + set codec), `coop.colony.CoopExpeditionWarningSync` (host scan + reconcile plan + `SectorWarningWorld`), `coop.colony.CoopExpeditionWarningIntel` (the mod's first custom intel plugin), plus the replicator wiring and two new pump ticks (`tickColonyIncome`, `tickExpeditionWarnings`, own profiler sections next to `replicator.colonyLifecycle`). **File-list deviations:** management got its own class rather than growing `CoopColonySync` past 1400 lines (same package, same conventions, `CoopColonySync`'s M3 seam note still holds for the lifecycle payload), and the message list gained a **sixth** type the plan did not name — `COLONY_INCOME`, see the income model below. **No new interaction gating was built:** the Phase 10 gate is a global first-come lockout on all dialogs, so two players can never be inside colony screens at once and concurrency is solved by construction. *(**Corrected 2026-09-04:** the command-tab colony screen is not a dialog and was never covered. Gating was added: while either player has the `OUTPOSTS` tab open, the pump holds a claim on `coop:colony-management` and the other player sees "Remote player is interacting: colony management" and is bounced to INTEL.)* **`COLONY_MGMT` payload:** header line `H|reportId|marketId|actingPlayerId|freePort|immigrationClosed|immigrationIncentives|useStockpiles`, then one `I|industryId|aiCoreId|improved|buildState|upgradeId|specialItemId|specialItemData` per industry and one `Q|industryId|cost` per construction-queue entry **in queue order**; `CoopDelimited`-escaped, `CoopBaseRecord.encodeSet` convention. **The diff decides whether to send; the payload is absolute.** Open-time snapshot vs close-time snapshot, and only a difference mints a report — a dock at your own colony to sell ore ships nothing. What ships is the whole post-close state, so a duplicate delivery, the host's rebroadcast and a late arrival are all idempotent and a mirror that drifted converges on the next edit instead of compounding. Ledger is set-based like M1's (a report is a fresh absolute snapshot with a fresh id, not a value that oscillates); dedup key = `actingPlayerId + ":" + monotonic counter`, session-scoped. **No baseline, no report** — a close of a screen this client never opened (reconnect mid-screen, a synthesised dialog) must not overwrite the peer's canonical market with whatever this client holds. **The construction queue is the load-bearing field, and that was the surprise:** vanilla's build button does not add an industry, it appends to `market.getConstructionQueue()` and charges the player (`IndustryListPanel.dialogDismissed`); the industry only appears later when `Market.advance` sees an idle queue and calls `BaseIndustry.buildNextInQueue` (`BaseIndustry.java:606-652`). So replicating the queue makes the mirror start the same construction through its *own* vanilla code — "replicate outcomes, not RNG" applied to a deterministic process — and the queue is replaced wholesale rather than diffed because the order *is* the state. Upgrades do not ride the queue (the UI calls `startUpgrading()` immediately), which is why `buildState` exists. **Removal is `removeIndustry(id, null, false)`:** the enum is only `{LOCAL, REMOTE}` and neither is silent — `null` is, because `BaseIndustry.getCargoForInteractionMode` returns null for it (`:690-696`) so no AI core or special item is returned to any cargo, and credits are never refunded by `removeIndustry` at all (the UI refunds, before calling it). **The upgrade race is why `upgradeId` is on the wire:** a finished upgrade is a *different industry id*, so a report of "heavyindustry, UPGRADING → orbitalworks" arriving at a mirror that already finished would look like "add heavy industry, delete orbital works" — a downgrade caused purely by being ahead. A reported upgrading industry whose target is already present is treated as satisfied and the target is protected from removal. **Build progress is deliberately not replicated** (accepted divergence, in `starsector-runtime-limitations.md`): `getBuildOrUpgradeProgress()` reads `0` while disrupted (`BaseIndustry.java:491-498`), absolute days need a `BaseIndustry` cast, and the `buildTime` field is unreadable during an upgrade (`getBuildTime()` returns the *spec* value). It self-heals with a bound — the report-says-finished direction forces `finishBuildingOrUpgrading()` on the lagging mirror, and the reverse is deliberately *not* forced because restarting a finished build would unapply a working industry to re-run a timer. **Captured surface:** industry set, per-industry AI core / improved flag / build state / upgrade target / special item (id+data), the ordered queue with costs, and four colony toggles (free port, immigration closed, immigration incentives, use-stockpiles-for-shortages). **Not captured — the admin:** `Market.getAdmin()` is not a pure read (it fabricates a default governor and swaps in the player person on a player-owned market, so a snapshot pass would itself mutate the market), and writing it back needs a live `PersonAPI` *instance* — ids are not accepted, `AdminData` compares by reference — plus a matching `CharacterDataAPI.getAdmins()` entry with its market back-pointer set, or the assignment UI desyncs and one governor can be assigned to two colonies. This mod does not replicate the person graph, so half-replicating it would be worse than not. **Not captured — the tariff:** faction level (`FactionSpecAPI.setTariffFraction`), not colony state. Cargo/stockpiles stay Phase 12's, conditions stay M1/M2/DECIV's. **Income — local-half deduction, no money on the wire.** The trap: `CoreScript.reportEconomyMonthEnd` filters on `market.isPlayerOwned()`, which is a flag on the market and not "owned by *this* client" (`CoreScript.java:1322`), so since M2 each engine already pays its own player the *whole* colony net and a "host wires the guest half" design would pay 150%. Instead each engine reads its own settled report, splits it 50/50 and deducts the half it does not keep; credits are per-player local state under the same rule salvage and raid loot follow, so nothing is transferred and the two clients agree because `CoopRewardSplitter` rounds identically (`localShare = total/2` truncating toward zero, remainder to the peer, so the parts always sum exactly — including for negative totals, where each side eats half the loss). **MonthlyReport evidence:** at the moment `EconomyTickListener.reportEconomyMonthEnd()` fires, `CoreScript` has *already* rolled the report over and paid the player (`:1113-1127`), so the settled month is `SharedData.getData().getPreviousReport()` with `computeTotals()` already run and `getCurrentReport()` is a fresh empty one. The reader walks `getRoot().getChildren().get(MonthlyReport.OUTPOSTS)` and sums `totalIncome - totalUpkeep` over children where `node.custom instanceof MarketAPI && isPlayerOwned()` — the same identification vanilla's own income panel uses. It deliberately does **not** call `getNode`/`getColoniesNode`/`getMarketNode`, which *create* the node they cannot find (`MonthlyReport.java:169-176`) and would silently grow the report tree. Excluded by anchoring at `OUTPOSTS`: fleet payroll (`FLEET` subtree, per-client by construction), storage fees at NPC markets (also `MarketAPI`-tagged, which is exactly why the anchor matters), admin salaries (`ADMIN`, a personal cost like payroll), custom production (per-engine `Random`) and last month's debt. A **timestamp guard** on the settled report prevents a double split, which is not theoretical: `CoreScript` returns early without rolling over while the tutorial is in progress (`:1074-1076`), so the "settled" report can be one already split. The deduction is clamped at zero exactly as vanilla clamps its own payment (`:1123-1127`). `COLONY_INCOME` (host→guest, `netCredits` + `colonyCount`) carries the host's canonical figure for **one** purpose — a log-level drift line, matched against the guest's own total in whichever order the two arrive. Correcting from it would mean transferring credits, which is the design this replaces. Banner is `Coop: colony income split - kept 12,500 of 25,000 credits.` (ASCII, ROOT-locale grouping so the separator is always a comma; a losing month gets its own "colony losses split - paid" wording), queued and flushed from the pump because a month can end on a frame with no `CampaignUIAPI`, and suppressed whenever the rounded total is zero — a superset of the specified "no colonies and no money" rule, chosen because "kept 0 of 0" is noise either way. **Expedition warnings — set-reconciled like `BASE_SET`:** host scans on a 1 s tick, hashes the set order-independently, and broadcasts only on change; guest reconciles on a 5 s tick and on arrival. Records are `kind|factionId|targetMarketId|targetName|etaDays|status`, identity `(kind, factionId, targetMarketId)`. **The ETA is bucketed to whole days on capture and that is load-bearing** — the live value is a float that moves every frame, so an unbucketed hash would rebroadcast the whole set at frame rate for the life of an expedition. Empty set is a legitimate value that clears everything; a scan that could read *neither* hierarchy returns null ("no reading this poll") rather than an empty set that would tell the guest to drop every warning. **Scan coverage, and the correction the spec needed:** 0.98a has two *disjoint* hierarchies. `RaidIntel` gives clean target markets only for `PunitiveExpeditionIntel.getTarget()` and `HegemonyInspectionIntel.getTarget()`; a plain `RaidIntel` (pirate-base raids) exposes only `getSystem()`, and in unmodded 0.98a it can never target a player colony anyway because `PirateBaseIntel.startRaid` hard-returns on any system containing a player market ("actually just no raids against the player, period - that's handled by Colony Crises") — it is covered by deriving player-owned markets in the system, four lines, for the modded case. The real gap was the other hierarchy: **every colony-crisis attack descends from `FleetGroupIntel`/`GenericRaidFGI`, not `RaidIntel`**, so a `RaidIntel`-only scan would have missed the most common modern threat entirely. Those resolve through `getParams().raidParams.allowedTargets` (filtered to player-owned) with the markets of `getRaidAction().getWhere()` as the blockade-shape fallback, and ETA from `getETAUntil(GenericRaidFGI.PAYLOAD_ACTION)`. `HostileActivityEventIntel` itself is a progress-bar event, not a target — it is the attacks it spawns that are scanned. Not covered: a `FleetGroupIntel` that is not a `GenericRaidFGI` (base game: only `TestFleetGroupIntel`) has no readable target. **Intel plugin — minimal overrides:** `getName` (widened from `protected`), `addBulletPoints(info, mode, isUpdate, tc, initPad)`, `createSmallDescription`, `getIntelTags` (super + `INTEL_MILITARY` + `INTEL_COLONIES` + faction id), `getSortString`, `getIcon`, `getFactionForUIColors`, `getMapLocation`, `advanceImpl`, `notifyEnded`. Icon is the threatening faction's crest — always a loaded sprite, what both vanilla raid hierarchies use — falling back to `null`, which the contract explicitly allows ("40x40, no icon if null"), never to a settings sprite key that might not exist. **Lifecycle:** the entry persists in the guest's save, so every field is a primitive or a `String` (XStream runs no constructors or field initialisers on load, and enum fields serialise by constant name); the enums are stored as names and re-parsed defensively. It registers itself as a sector script in its constructor (`BaseIntelPlugin` does not, and without one `advanceImpl` never runs — same as `RaidIntel.java:87`) and removes itself in `notifyEnded`. **Self-expire:** ends itself after 10 in-game days without a coop touch; a live session refreshes every mirrored entry on every reconcile pass, several times a minute, so the timer only runs out when nobody is left to refresh it. Session `dispose` clears all entries outright; the timer is the backstop for a teardown that never ran (crash, or a save taken mid-session and loaded solo). **Divergences documented** in `starsector-runtime-limitations.md`: the guest's `HostileActivityManager` is deliberately not suppressed (its fleet spawns die via the suppressed `RouteManager`, but its meter and event progress diverge — the mirrored warning is the authoritative inbound-attack signal), and the construction-progress drift above. 89 new tests (23 `CoopColonyManagementTest`, 21 `CoopColonyIncomeTest`, 23 `CoopExpeditionWarningTest`, 14 `CoopColonyMgmtReplicatorTest`, 8 `CoopRewardSplitterTest`), suite 1019 → 1108 green. `MonthlyReport` is a plain data class with a public no-arg constructor, so the income tests run against the real one rather than a fake of it. **Correction 2026-08-25 (live two-instance session, three defects):** **(1) The diff-on-close trigger is structurally unreliable and is no longer the capture.** Live, the host toggled free port across several docked visits and most produced no `COLONY_MGMT` at all — vanilla's market-close callback fires on some trade-screen exit paths and not others (the quirk Phase 18 already recorded) — and the colony screen reached from the command/intel UI docks nowhere, so it fires *neither* callback and could never be captured by any amount of fixing. **A change poll is now the primary capture** (`CoopColonyManagement.Poll`, wired as `CoopCampaignReplicator.tickColonyManagement` + `replicator.colonyManagement` profiler section next to `tickColonyLifecycle`, `COLONY_MGMT_POLL_INTERVAL_MILLIS = 2000`): every 2 s each player-owned, in-economy, non-hyperspace market is re-captured with the *same* `capture(...)` and compared by `State.contentHash()` — everything `sameStateAs` compares, minus the `reportId`/ `actingPlayerId` header identity, as the content itself rather than a digest (a colony's state is a handful of short records; hashing to an int would only buy collisions). Diff-on-close stays as the low-latency assist for the docked case vanilla does report, and **both routes send through one helper** so the ledger entry and the known-synced hash are always taken together. **Suppression:** “known synced” is updated on send *and* on apply, because an engine-driven transition (a queue entry popping into a build, a build finishing) lands on both engines at once, both polls speak once, and each inbound state is then content-identical — `applyToEngine` skips the engine work on a content match and the hash marking stops the echo. **Session edge:** `armBaseline()` on the session-active edge (in `registerOn`, the canonical edge) drops every hash; the first tick after it is asymmetric — the **host** re-sends every colony unconditionally to heal divergence accumulated while the channel was down, the **guest** records without sending because the host is canonical on reconnect. `dispose` resets it. **(2) The mirror's reconcile was throwing and the guard swallowed it.** Guest log: `NullPointerException: Cannot invoke "IndustrySpecAPI.getBuildTime()" because "upgrade" is null` out of `BaseIndustry.startUpgrading` (`:575-579`). Root cause is one vanilla override read at face value on both ends: `PopulationAndInfrastructure.isUpgrading()` returns **true for any colony below its maximum size** (`:613-617`) — that is colony *growth*, not an upgrade — so the capture shipped “population is UPGRADING” for every ordinary colony, and `population` has an empty `upgrade` column in `industries.csv`, so the mirror upgraded it into nothing. `BaseIndustry.canUpgrade()` is a hardcoded `return true` (`:1627`) and guarded nothing. Fixed at **both** ends with `CoopColonyManagement.liveBuildState(Industry)`: an `isUpgrading()` reading only counts as `UPGRADING` when the industry's spec actually has an upgrade to become, so growth captures as `NONE`; a real first-time build is untouched (`startBuilding()` leaves `upgradeId` null, so `isUpgrading()` is false). The apply side reads the same classifier — which also kills the mirror-only “construction completed” message and the premature `buildNextInQueue` that a `NONE` report used to fire against that false `isBuilding()` — and refuses `startUpgrading()` outright when the local spec has no upgrade. **(3) Fault isolation.** The `try` sat around the whole industry loop, so the throwing industry (population, first in the list) starved every industry behind it — live, the guest's colony never got its spaceport at all.
