- Create `src/main/java/coop/net/CoopCadenceController.java` (host-side per-link tier selection: thresholds + hysteresis; TCP-fallback floor pin; adaptive interpolation-delay formula)
- Modify `src/main/java/coop/net/CoopMessages.java` (datagram envelope: `epoch` + `sentGameTime`; later the tier field on `LINK_STATUS`), `coop/fleet/CoopNpcFleetMotion.java` + `CoopFleetSnapshot.java` senders (redundant-sample packing; later tier-driven emit interval), `coop/fleet/CoopFleetMirror.java` (kinematic drive: delete `driveMovement`'s `setMoveDestinationOverride` and `LOCATION_CORRECTION_EVERY`; velocity-derived facing with speed floor), `CoopFleetMirrorRegistry.java` (route motion through the interpolator; per-frame evaluate), `coop/net/CoopNetPump.java` (epoch watermark drop on the datagram drain), `coop/net/CoopLinkQuality.java` (feed the controller), `coop/ui/CoopLinkHud.java` + `coop/ui/CoopSessionIntel.java` (tier display)
- Unit tests: epoch watermark (reordered datagram dropped, equal accepted), Hermite math (tangent scaling; arc-length fallback; straight-line reduces to lerp), cursor time-scaling (dead zone, no ping-pong at a noisy boundary, pause dt=0 freeze), starvation ladder (cap → decay-to-stop → re-seat without teleport), snap cases (`locationId` change → hard cut; two-radius backstop), redundant-sample dedup, `1 − exp(−dt/τ)` frame-rate independence, hysteresis state machine, TCP-fallback floor pin

**Steps:**

Commit `feat: mirror snapshot interpolation`. *(BUILT 2026-08-24, `9e4f935`: `CoopMotionInterpolator` + shared `CoopMotionTimeline` cursor, `driveMovement`/`LOCATION_CORRECTION_EVERY` deleted, velocity-derived facing with 5 su/s floor, set-fed samples via the `NPC_FLEET_SET` stamp; 22 unit tests. As-built calibration: delay 200 ms, dead zone ±100 ms, timescale ×1.02/×0.96, drift EMA 1 s, cursor re-seat on forward drift only with the cursor bounded 1 s ahead of target (corrected 2026-09-04: re-seating on |drift| made a starved stream overrun and snap back once a second for the whole outage), extrapolation cap 250 ms, decay window 200 ms, teleport radius 2000 su, tangent guard 3× chord + 1 su, buffer cap 32. **Clean-link smoke VERIFIED in-game 2026-08-24** — orbit-pair glide smooth, ambient NPC motion smooth, zero coop errors across the session, coop frame cost ~0.1 ms avg with no frame over 16.7 ms, pause edge ~205 ms log-measured (after fixing a leftover Phase 18 `interactionDelayMs=1500` lever that the launch scripts had let persist in the host vmparams — scripts now strip all `-Dcoop.*`, commit `e4f8fc9`). **Shaped-loopback loss/reorder pass DONE 2026-09-03 as part of the M2 certification** (m2 12 % loss, m6 60 ms lag + 30 % reorder: no re-seats, no visible artifacts, guest speed-probe tail equal to the clean baseline); no radius or threshold re-tune was needed. **Speed-probe baseline (2026-08-24, `CoopMotionSpeedProbe`, diagnostics-gated, 10 s windows):** host ≈ 0.99–1.00 (one mostly-straight partner mirror), guest ≈ **1.08–1.11 — and that is the healthy clean-link reading, not a defect**: the ratio is biased ≥ 1.0 by construction (60 fps path integral vs 10 Hz chord sum measures any curve longer), plus su-scale Hermite bow on near-stationary orbiters whose engine velocity does not match their near-zero displacement — phantom path length, no net displacement, invisible at campaign scale and user-confirmed so. Judged not worth re-tuning a verified build; if the shaped pass ever needs it, the lever is tightening the tangent guard or lerping segments with chords under ~1 su. Compare future probe readings against this baseline, and watch for sustained readings **below ~0.97** — that direction would mean mirrors genuinely under-covering ground. **Baseline caveat (2026-08-25):** wire quantization (`1d0d53e`, positions/velocities snapped to 0.25 su at encode) landed after this measurement, so the interpolator now consumes snapped samples — near-zero orbiter velocities encode as 0 and should *shrink* the Hermite bow, likely pulling the guest ratio toward 1.0. Re-baseline at the next diagnostics session (the 12c smoke) and record the post-quantization numbers here; the ~0.97 under-covering threshold is unchanged. **Post-quantization re-baseline DONE 2026-08-25, two passes.** Pass 1 (pause-punctuated smoke session): sustained mean 1.072 — read at the time as quantization pulling the ratio down. Pass 2 (dedicated continuous run, 30 game days unpaused via `ss_advance_days`, 132 sustained windows): **median 1.087, mean 1.090, IQR 1.062–1.120, max 1.215** — squarely inside the original 1.08–1.11 band, so the pass-1 dip was the pause-punctuation artifact and **quantization did not materially change the ratio** (the predicted Hermite-bow shrink did not show). Under-covering: 1 of 132 windows at 0.928, isolated, none sustained — healthy. This is the standing baseline for future probe comparisons.)*
- [x] **M2:** implement `CoopCadenceController` + `LINK_STATUS` tier field + adaptive interpolation delay + HUD/intel/feed surfacing + TCP-fallback floor unification + game-time send cadence (FF rule); certify each shipped tier on the shaped matrix. Commit `feat: adaptive state-stream cadence`. *(BUILT 2026-09-03, `5f01a98`, 1798 → 1851 tests. **As built:** tiers 5 Hz/200 ms (FLOOR), 10 Hz/100 ms (DEFAULT), 20 Hz/50 ms (TOP, `TOP_TIER_ENABLED=false`, so the live ladder is {5, 10} and an announced 20 is refused back to 10). Host decides per link on the 1 s link tick from the RTT **p50** (new `CoopLinkQuality.medianRttMillis()` off the 32-sample ring; never p95 or the EWMA, per the WAN-smoke input), the 10 s loss window, the transport's own coalescing threshold as the backlog signal (`CoopNetService.outboundBacklogged()` = outbound queue ≥ 32 messages), and the fallback flag. Downshift immediately on any of loss ≥ 10 %, p50 ≥ 400 ms, backlog, fallback; upshift only after 30 s continuously clean (loss ≤ 3 %, p50 < 300 ms, no backlog, no fallback; one dirty second restarts the window; the band between the two threshold pairs holds the tier without accumulating). Announced as `LINK_STATUS.cadenceHz` (optional on parse, default 10) and pulled forward on every change; the guest applies the announced tier on receipt, its own local fallback pins FLOOR. **Fallback unification:** `applyStateStreamFallback` no longer sets any interval (`FALLBACK_STREAM_INTERVAL_MILLIS` deleted); the tier is the one mechanism, fallback is an input that pins FLOOR, so leaving the fallback returns the transport to UDP at once and the rate 30 s later through the clean window. **Adaptive delay:** jitter σ = EMA variance (α = 2/21, about 2 s at the default tier) of the wall-clock inter-arrival of fresh `FLEET_SNAPSHOT` datagrams, gaps > 2 s discarded; delay = `2 × interval + σ` clamped [150, 500] ms with interval = the peer's announced tier (the ceiling form in the first build, `025c374` removed it: frame scheduling alone gives 8 to 11 ms of σ on a clean loopback and the ceiling turned that into a whole extra interval, 300 ms instead of 200); clean default lands at 205 to 210 ms, clean floor at about 410 ms; a new value applies only when it differs from the applied one by ≥ 25 ms and any value holds ≥ 5 s, the first value of a session applies at once; a delay step goes through the timeline's ×1.02/×0.96 path, never the 1 s re-seat. **Redundancy depth 2** iff tier is FLOOR and the path is UDP and max(local loss, fresh peer-reported loss) ≥ 10 %; the motion chunker's budget is now `overhead + (depth + 1) × fullForm ≤ 1200 B` and a depth change re-packs from full form. **Surfacing:** HUD adds a `5 Hz` segment only off the default tier; intel "State stream" line carries the Hz for both samples; one feed line per change (`cadenceUp`/`cadenceDown` kinds on the 30 s limiter) and one intel event. No Phase 28 key. **Shaped-matrix certification PASSED 2026-09-03 03:06 to 03:38** (runbook and run log `tmp_ff_analysis\phase29\M2_CERT.md`; clumsy on loopback, both clients `-Diagnostics -Bridge`, host wiretap on): m1 clean 5 min: tier never left 10 Hz, no transitions, no coop WARN, depth 1, no HUD Hz segment; m2 drop 12 %: host to 5 Hz on `loss 10%` at +14 s, guest followed +1 s, depth 2 through the loss, back to 10 Hz on `clean 30 s` 35 s after the loss ended, depth back to 1; m3 lag 230 ms/way: host to 5 Hz on `rtt p50 481 ms` at +53 s, back about 80 s after the lag ended (the p50 ring spans 96 s of PING history, so RTT-driven transitions trail the event by 50 to 80 s where loss-driven ones take about 14 s; recorded as observation M2-O1, no action); m4 UDP block: fallback line and `cadence -> 5 Hz reason=tcp fallback` in the same millisecond, depth stayed 1 on TCP, UDP back about 9 s after the lift and the rate 30 s later; m5 three 20 s loss bursts 20 s apart: exactly one downshift and one upshift 42 s after the last burst, nothing in between; m6 lag 60 ms + 30 % reorder on `025c374`: delay widened to 236 to 243 ms and narrowed back to 211 to 217, zero re-seats, zero fallback, guest speed-probe median 1.09 with 2 of 22 windows above 1.15 and a 1.215 maximum, equal to the standing baseline's maximum and below m1's tail under the old 300 ms delay; the user saw the partner mirror move smoothly. Finding M2-F1 (the ceiling in the delay formula) found in the first minute and fixed in `025c374`, 1855 green.)*
- [ ] **M2 inputs from the Phase 20 real-Internet smoke (2026-09-02):** (1) the guest's motion speed-probe ratio ran 1.00 to 1.22 per 10 s window (median about 1.10, standing baseline 1.087) with the tunnel's RTT alternating between about 53 ms and 181 ms; the peaks sat in the lowest-movement windows, and the adaptive interpolation delay is the lever, so treat the tail above 1.15 as M2's acceptance signal on the shaped matrix. (2) RTT is measured by TCP PING/PONG across game frames, so a sample carries up to one frame interval per side on top of the network; a client whose frame rate is capped (the user's NVIDIA profile did it tonight; a background window does it too) inflates every sample it answers by its frame interval. The cadence pick must therefore key on the p50 with hysteresis, not the p95, or a capped client flaps the tier.
- [x] Document the frozen calibration values in this section; update the Phase 28 not-configurable list's cadence entry if wording needs the tier mechanism named. *(2026-09-03: values are in the M2 step above; the Phase 28 entry needs no rewording, cadence stays not-configurable and the tier is picked by code.)*

**Acceptance:**

- On a shaped 100 ms / 2% loss / jitter link, mirrored fleets move visually continuously: no per-snapshot stepping, no periodic correction pop, no overshoot/rubber-banding; a fleet that jumps or despawns cuts instantly, never glides.
- **Orbit-pair scenario (2026-08-20, the live worst case):** host and guest parked in orbit at the same planet see each other *glide* along the drift, and a stationary-drift mirror produces zero hard correction snaps on a clean link. A sustained-burn traveling fleet's mirror tracks it without the ~1 Hz catch-up teleport.
- A single lost datagram produces no visible artifact (the redundant samples cover it); a reordered datagram is dropped at the watermark, never applied.
- A packet-starved mirror coasts for at most ~250 ms, decays to a stop, and parks; it never diverges unboundedly from the last authoritative position. On traffic resume it re-seats through time-scaling rather than a teleport, up to a point: a cursor more than 1 s behind the target is re-seated outright, by design, and the starved direction now matches (2026-09-04).
- Shared pause freezes mirrors exactly where they are (game-clock cursor, dt = 0) and unpause resumes without a hop.
- Tier changes occur only between certified tiers, per link, with hysteresis (no flapping on a noisy link), and every change is announced (feed message + HUD + intel). With UDP blocked, the TCP fallback presents as the pinned floor tier — one code path.
- `TIME_SNAPSHOT`, battle status, and all TCP semantic streams are untouched at every tier; Phase 28's registry gains no key and the not-configurable list still has zero knobs.
- On a clean LAN at the default tier, the session is behaviorally identical to pre-Phase-29 apart from the smoothing itself; the only wire differences are the two envelope fields and the redundant samples (documented above), the tier never leaves default, and all existing unit + smoke tests stay green.

**Sources (2026-08-24 research pass):**

- Fiedler, *Snapshot Interpolation* / *State Synchronization* — gafferongames.com (10 Hz budget, 3×-interval buffer rule, the failed 200 ms extrapolation experiment, error-decay constants)
- Valve, *Source Multiplayer Networking* — `cl_interp 0.1` = 2 intervals, `cl_extrapolate_amount 0.25`, `cl_smoothtime 0.1`
- Mirror `SnapshotInterpolation.cs` — bufferTime = 2× interval, ±1-interval dead zone, ×1.02/×0.96 time-scaling, 1 s drift EMA, jitter-adaptive multiplier
- Unity Netcode for Entities / NGO docs — extrapolation cap 20 ticks, kinematic non-authority rigidbodies, 1 Hz axial resync as interpolator input (not a teleport)
- Unreal networked movement docs + CMC defaults — simulated proxies set location/rotation/velocity from replication, `NetworkSimulatedSmoothLocationTime 0.100`, 256/384 uu two-radius snap, adaptive net frequency 2 s/7 s/30%
- Murphy, *Believable Dead Reckoning for Networked Games* (Game Engine Gems 2) — projective velocity blending, one-interval blend window, acceleration-noise warning (kept as the reference for the starvation-ladder correction math)
- Ryan & Oliver, DIS dead-reckoning modelling + IEEE 1278.1 Table 25 — second-order-vs-first-order PDU counts, threshold/heartbeat defaults (grounds the no-threshold-sending non-goal)
- Overwatch GDC 2017 — ~2-tick interp delay, buffer control by ~5% time dilation
- Gambetta, *Entity Interpolation* — render-in-the-past rationale for non-predicted entities

## Phase 30: Agent Bridge + Starsector MCP (dev tooling, V1 since 2026-08-25)

> **Why this phase exists (user decision 2026-08-25):** the Phase 12c smoke checklist is 10 items, and 7 of them are state-equality or state-over-time assertions that a machine checks better than eyes. The user is not always available to drive two instances, so this phase gives the agent a query/act channel into both running games: a dormant in-mod TCP bridge per instance plus an external MCP server wrapping both bridges into tools. **Scope verdict from the feasibility assessment (do not re-litigate): read + diff + setup verbs only.** No UI synthesis, no OS input injection, no screenshots — checks that exist to exercise UI-listener wiring (buy/sell/hire close-diffs, survey-dialog flow, bar-offer accept) stay manual or unit-tested, because driving the engine underneath the UI tests a different code path than the one the check exists to verify. This is a dev tool: dormant without its system property, never part of release behavior; Phase 23 gains a dormancy-confirmation line, not a strip.

**Architecture (all constraints verified by the existing transport — mirror `CoopNetService` patterns exactly):**

- **In-mod bridge** (`coop.debug.CoopAgentBridge`): dormant unless `-Dcoop.debug.bridge=<port>` is set (absent/0/unparsable = no socket ever, no log noise; read the property the way `CoopDebug` reads its flags). Binds **127.0.0.1 only**. `java.nio` non-blocking `ServerSocketChannel` + one `SocketChannel` client at a time, accepted and serviced entirely inside `advance()` on the campaign thread — **no threads, no Selector, no `java.io`** (sandbox; byte-array framing + UTF-8 like the coop TCP pump). Installed as its own **transient** script at campaign load (NOT persistent — XStream; and NOT inside the coop pump's session lifecycle, because the bridge must answer before/without an active coop session).
- **Protocol:** newline-delimited JSON, request `{"id":<int>,"cmd":"<verb>","args":{...}}` → response `{"id":<int>,"ok":true,"data":{...}}` or `{"id":<int>,"ok":false,"error":"<Class>: <message>"}`. Every command runs synchronously during `advance()` inside `try/catch (RuntimeException | LinkageError)` — a malformed or crashing command yields an error response, never a game crash. Cap dispatch at ~4 commands/frame; responses are single JSON lines (localhost, size is fine).
- **MCP server** (`tools/starsector-mcp/`, **not** inside the mod jar): Node ≥18 + `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`, stdio transport (chosen for implementation reliability). Maps instance names → ports (`host`→7801, `guest`→7802, overridable via env). **All diffing is server-side** — dump both instances, structural JSON diff, return differences + counts; the mod stays dumb. Ships a `README.md` with a `.mcp.json` registration example for Claude Code.
- **Launch integration:** `scripts/launch-host.ps1` / `launch-guest.ps1` gain a `-Bridge` switch that appends `-Dcoop.debug.bridge=7801/7802` through the existing `-ExtraJvmProps` path (the catch-all `-Dcoop.*` strip already resets stale values).

**Command set v1** (mod side; queries reuse the replication capture code — that is the whole trick, the serializers already exist):

| verb | args | returns / does | reuses |
|---|---|---|---|
| `status` | — | role, sessionActive, clock date+timestamp, paused, player fleet {locationId, x, y} | session state + clock |
| `fleets` | `locationId?` | per fleet: coopFleetId/engineId, name, faction, x, y, vx, vy, transponder, actionText, members [{variantId, cr, hullFraction}] | NPC set capture + mirror registry |
| `market` | `marketId` | full stock incl. ship details, specials, hireables. **Host: dock-equivalent** (runs the same `ensureOpenMarketStocked` a real dock runs — document, don't "fix"). Guest: raw current cargo; never-docked → explicit `"stocked":false`, not an error | `captureOpenMarketStock` + hireable capture |
| `barpool` | — | ordered offer list (barEventId, seed, shownAt) + `PortsideBarData` render order | `CoopBarPoolCapture` |
| `survey` | `systemId\|"all"` | planetId → {level, ruinsExplored} | the 12c-D collector |
| `visibility` | `fleetId?` | host-vs-guest detectability dump | `CoopFleetVisibilityProbe` |
| `teleport` | `entityId` **or** `x, y, locationId` (mutually exclusive) | player fleet relocation. `entityId` resolves any entity in the sector (`SectorAPI.getEntityById`, then a location walk) and parks the fleet at `radius + 200` on +x, so a planet target needs no orbit arithmetic. **Crossing locations runs `SectorAPI.doHyperspaceTransition`, not a raw re-parent** — the raw move skipped `setCurrentLocation`, and `CampaignEngine.advance` hands player input only to `getCurrentLocation()`, which is why a cross-system teleport left the fleet unable to fly (live defect, 2026-08-25). Response carries `transition` (`jump`/`local`) and `pending`; the jump is a frame script and does **not** advance while paused | — |
| `pause` | `on\|off` | via the shared pause coordinator (guest = its intent; host = authority) | Phase 11 |
| `ability` | `abilityId` | `playerFleet.getAbility(id).activate()` — **UI-faithful**: same engine path as the toolbar (isPlayerFleet → `reportPlayerActivatedAbility` → mod listener → `ABILITY_ACTIVATE`) | — |
| `setcr` | `value, memberIndex\|"all"` | `RepairTracker.setCR` on own fleet (CR-recovery drill setup) | — |
| `give` | `commodityId?, qty?, credits?` | dev cargo/credit grants for setup | — |
| `objective` | `entityId, factionId` | ownership flip via the same `Objectives.control`-equivalent the dialog runs — poll-faithful | — |
| `surveyset` | `planetId, level` | engine-level set — poll-faithful (NOT dialog-listener-faithful; the dialog path stays manual) | 12c-D apply helpers |

**Added after v1** (the table above is the v1 set; these landed against live-run needs and the registry test in `CoopAgentCommandsTest` is the authority on the current list):

| verb | args | returns / does | added |
|---|---|---|---|
| `markets` | — | economy index (marketId, name, factionId, size, locationId); enumeration only, never stocks | 2026-08-25, `1f4aed2` (14 total) |
| `expedition` | `factionId?` | **host-only**: forces one punitive expedition via `PunitiveExpeditionManager.createExpedition` (the manager's own public call, `MAX_CONCURRENT` aside), so the Phase 24 milestone-3 warning check does not wait months of game time for an organic one. Auto-picks the first faction with a live reason, preferring `ANTI_FREE_PORT`; skips factions already running one. Success read off `PunExData.intel` (the call is void and bails silently); the intel self-registers and `CoopExpeditionWarningSync`'s `RaidIntel` scan mirrors it to the guest on its next 1 s poll. Refusals name the cause, incl. the one precondition a caller can create: free port on a player colony | 2026-08-25 (15 total) |
| `colonizable` | `limit?, maxLy?, neutralOnly?` | **query, any role** (local read, nothing written): the uncolonized planets nearest the local player fleet, so the Phase 24 smoke can name a colony target instead of hunting the map for one. Per planet: `planetId`, `name`, `type`, `gasGiant`, `systemId`/`systemName`, location-local `x`/`y` (a `teleport` argument — planets orbit, so hand-deriving the pair from the orbit definition is both work and wrong), `marketsInSystem`, `distanceLy` + in-system `distanceSu`, `hazard`, `surveyLevel`, `unexploredRuins`, sorted `conditions`. **`marketsInSystem` counts *economy* markets in the planet's location and `neutralOnly` keeps only the 0 rows** — "a system nobody is in" in one query rather than a cross-reference against the `markets` dump. It is a live-colony count by construction: condition-only markets are never in `EconomyAPI` and `DecivTracker` calls `getEconomy().removeMarket` on the way out. The filter runs before `limit`, so `limit:3, neutralOnly:true` is three neutral planets, not the three nearest with the occupied ones struck out. Sorted LY → in-system → id, and the condition list is sorted too, so `ss_diff` on it is a real worldgen check. **Filter = vanilla's, and the authority is not rules.csv:** the colonize button is disabled by the core UI class `PlanetSurveyPanel`, and two of its four location gates (`system_abyssal`, `StarSystem.isDeepSpace()`) appear in *no* rule and in *no* API source — a rules.csv-only reading would have missed them. Shipped set: non-star planet + `market.isPlanetConditionMarketOnly()` (the uncolonized flag, same gate `CoopColonySync` captures on and the one `surveyAddOptionPerformedAlready` requires), system not hyperspace / not deep space / not `SYSTEM_ABYSSAL` / not `SYSTEM_CUT_OFF_FROM_HYPER` (`CoreCampaignPluginImpl:207-209` derives `$systemCutOffFromHyper` from that tag; `GateExplosionScript:80-84` sets it precisely to block colonies), plus `TEMPORARY_LOCATION` as the one deliberate step past vanilla (throwaway abyssal-encounter systems — redundant today, insurance against a modded one). **Gas giants are in** (vanilla colonizes them; `isGasGiant()` is not read anywhere in the colonize path) and **`THEME_HIDDEN` is not filtered** (off-map-until-found; the vanilla systems carrying it are blocked by abyssal/deep-space instead). **Survey level and unexplored ruins are reported, not filtered** — both are real vanilla gates (`FULL` required; "The ruins on this planet must be explored…") but both are ones the run clears itself, the first via `surveyset`, so filtering them would hide exactly the targets the smoke may set up | 2026-08-25 (16 total) |
| `landmarks` | `kinds?, limit?, maxLy?` | **query, any role**: the notable objects a colony site is chosen relative to, nearest the local player fleet first. Five kinds — `hypershunt` (`Tags.CORONAL_TAP`), `cryosleeper` (`Tags.CRYOSLEEPER`), `gate` (`Tags.GATE`), `stable_location` (`Tags.STABLE_LOCATION`) and `gate_hauler`, which has **no identifying tag at all** (its four are `has_interaction_dialog`/`salvageable`/`neutrino_high`/`not_random_mission_target`, every one shared with cryosleepers or ordinary salvage) and is matched on spec id `derelict_gatehauler` instead. Per row: kind, entityId, name, spec `type`, systemId/systemName, `hyperspace`, location-local `x`/`y` (same `teleport`-ready meaning as `colonizable`'s), `distanceLy` + in-system `distanceSu`, plus per-kind extras. Sorted LY → SU → kind → entityId. **Not all one-per-sector:** hypershunts and cryosleepers are exactly 2 each (`MiscellaneousThemeGenerator:651-652` hard-sets `numTaps=2`; `DerelictThemeGenerator:131` `numCryo=2`), the gate hauler exactly 1, but gates are 15-20 plus a second pass (`settings.json` `minNonCoreGatesInSector`/`maxNonCoreGatesInSector`/`minGatesToAddOnSecondPass`) and stable locations commoner still — hence `kinds` and a default limit of 25. **Colony-relevance constants are read off the engine, never copied:** `benefitRangeLy` = `ItemEffectsRepo.CORONAL_TAP_LIGHT_YEARS` (binary gate, `BaseInstallableItemEffect:152-159`) and `Cryorevival.MAX_BONUS_DIST_LY`, plus `minBenefitMult` = `Cryorevival.MIN_BONUS_MULT` (graded, `Cryorevival:233-246`); all three are non-final `public static` fields, 10 / 10 / 0.1 in stock 0.98a, and the field is omitted rather than guessed if a read fails. The cross-reference stays the caller's: vanilla measures those radii **from the colony**, not from the fleet, and only over discovered `HypershuntIntel`/`CryosleeperIntel`. `usable` mirrors vanilla's `$usable` `contains()` check (guardian beaten / tap repaired). Gate extras `active`/`scanned`/`gatesActive`/`playerCanUseGates`; `active` is the weakest (plugin `madeActive`, only set inside `advance()`, so a never-loaded gate reads false), and the sector-wide pair is read from sector memory and **not** through `GateEntityPlugin.areGatesActive()`, which ORs in a Janus Device in the local player's cargo and would make one sector diff differently per client. **No `occupied` field on `stable_location`, and that is not an omission:** `Objectives.build` (`Objectives.java:370-393`) creates the relay/array/buoy as a new entity, copies the orbit across and then `removeEntity`s the stable location, so one that still exists is free by construction. Story one-offs (Ziggurat, Alpha Site, red planet, Nameless Rock, Galatia) excluded — memory-flag-identified, several absent at worldgen, none affects siting | 2026-08-25 (17 total) |

**Explicit non-verbs (out of scope, by design):** market buy/sell, officer hire, bar-offer accept, market open/close — each exists as a *check* precisely because a UI listener drives it (`PlayerMarketTransaction`, close-diff hire claims, snapshot-on-open); a bridge version would test the wrong path and green-light broken wiring. No screenshots, no input injection, no save/load control.

**MCP tools (server side):** `ss_status(instance)`, `ss_dump(instance, what, args)`, `ss_diff(what, args)` (both instances + structural diff), `ss_act(instance, verb, args)`, `ss_advance_days(days)` (orchestrated server-side: unpause → poll clock → pause; the mod adds nothing).

**Automation map against the 12c smoke checklist** (what this phase converts): (1) bar-offer equality AUTOMATED, claim race manual; (2) ship-listing equality (D-mods/CR/price) AUTOMATED; (3) officer/merc/admin equality + the 5-day persistence drill AUTOMATED, the hire click-through manual; (4) refitted sell-back manual; (5) interdiction pulse AUTOMATED (`ability` is UI-faithful; assert via `fleets` diff + rep delta in logs); (6) distress call AUTOMATED (`ability` + `ss_advance_days` + `fleets`); (7) objective + survey replication AUTOMATED (the poll watches state, not UI); (8) motion feel MANUAL + the speed-probe re-baseline (already diagnostics-automated); (9) stealth: `visibility` dump AUTOMATED, feel manual; (10) CR recovery AUTOMATED (`setcr` low on guest → advance days → host `fleets` shows the mirror climbing — the exact `061bc22` ratchet regression). **Residual manual list for the user's next short session: claim race, sell-back + hire click-throughs, motion/stealth feel pass.**

**Agent prompt:**

```text
Implement Phase 30 from COOP_MP_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_V1.md exactly as specced: the dormant in-mod CoopAgentBridge (java.nio non-blocking, campaign-thread-only, 127.0.0.1, property-gated, transient script, no java.io/reflection) with the v1 command table, and the tools/starsector-mcp Node stdio server with server-side diffing. Reuse the existing capture serializers for every query verb — do not write parallel state readers. Implement the non-verbs list as a refusal (unknown command error), not as TODOs. Mirror CoopNetService's framing and CoopDebug's gating patterns. Unit tests: property-absent = no socket; codec round-trip; per-command error isolation (a throwing handler yields ok:false and the next command still runs); dispatch cap per frame. Then wire the -Bridge switch into both launch scripts and write tools/starsector-mcp/README.md with the .mcp.json example. gradlew clean test build green; commit mod-side and tools-side as separate commits.
```

**Files:**

- Create `src/main/java/coop/debug/CoopAgentBridge.java` (socket, framing, dispatch loop, per-frame cap)
- Create `src/main/java/coop/debug/CoopAgentCommands.java` (command registry — handlers take a small context interface so they unit-test with fakes)
- Modify `src/main/java/coop/CoopModPlugin.java` (install the transient bridge script at campaign load when the property is set)
- Modify `scripts/launch-host.ps1`, `scripts/launch-guest.ps1` (`-Bridge` switch)
- Create `tools/starsector-mcp/package.json`, `tools/starsector-mcp/index.js`, `tools/starsector-mcp/README.md`
- Modify `README_DEV.md` (bridge ports, MCP registration, the non-verbs rationale)
- Create `src/test/java/coop/debug/CoopAgentBridgeTest.java`, `CoopAgentCommandsTest.java`

**Steps:**

- [x] Mod-side bridge + commands + tests; `gradlew clean test build` green (943 tests); commit `feat: dormant agent bridge (-Dcoop.debug.bridge)` = `c51ee37`. *(2026-08-25. As-built notes: `catch` clause adds `JSONException` — the bundled 2010 org.json declares it checked; `fleets` enumerates fleets itself but captures every field through the shared capturers (`CoopSensorSync`/`CoopNpcActionTextCapture`/`CoopFleetSnapshotFactory`) because the replicator's set-capture path skips coop mirrors and feeds the motion smoother — both wrong for a read-only diff; floats quantized to 3 decimals in responses so the structural diff doesn't flag float tails; named non-verbs get a "stays manual" refusal distinct from unknown-command; `install()` closes a stale listener before the dormancy check so a second game load in one process can re-bind; reuse doors = narrow public `*ForBridge` facades on `CoopCampaignReplicator`/`CoopNetPump` delegating to the still-private originals.)*
- [x] MCP server + launch-script switch + README_DEV; commit `feat: starsector-mcp dev tools` = `4ed9a77`. *(2026-08-25. 20 mock-bridge tests via `npm test`; SDK pinned 1.30.0; env overrides `STARSECTOR_MCP_{HOST_PORT,GUEST_PORT,ADDRESS,TIMEOUT_MS}`; `ss_diff` adds optional numeric `tolerance` (default 0) and `missing`/`note` fields on difference entries; `ss_advance_days` budget derived from the 10 s/game-day clock rate and re-pauses on every exit path; `pause` wire shape = `{"on":true|false}`.)*
- [x] Live check with both instances bridged: `ss_diff(market)` at a shared dock returns an empty diff; `ss_act(guest, ability, interdiction_pulse)` produces the host-side `ABILITY_ACTIVATE` log line and visible scatter in `ss_dump(fleets)`. *(**PASS 2026-08-25**, Jangala: market diff equal at 194 leaves/side after the guest's snapshot-on-open (the pre-open dump correctly showed the guest raw/empty — the documented dock-equivalent asymmetry); interdiction landed host-side (`ABILITY_ACTIVATE seq=133`, `ACTIVATE_ON_MIRROR`, rep hit on 2 fleets). Run through the lib modules directly (`tmp_ff_analysis\ss-cli.mjs`) because the `.mcp.json` registration only mounts on the next session start — same code path as the MCP tools minus the stdio wrapper.)*
- [x] Run the automated 7-item portion of the 12c smoke via MCP end-to-end; record results in the 12c smoke step; leave the 3-item manual residue listed there. *(**RUN 2026-08-25, all 7 PASS** — full results + 3 findings recorded in the 12c smoke step. Tooling nits found during the run — **all fixed same day in `1f4aed2`**: `ss_diff` ignores `role`/`engineId`/`lines` by default (`ignore` option); player fleets key as `player:<playerId>` on both instances; the `survey` single-system lookup resolves enumerator ids; `ability` takes `on:true|false` for idempotent activate/deactivate. Same commit adds run-driven upgrades: `status.pause` intent breakdown + `blockingScreenOpen`, a `markets` enumeration verb (14 total), structured `visibility.view` (guest actual vs host estimate, directly diffable), `ss_advance_days` stall diagnosis naming what holds the clock, and setup-script seeding of help-popups-off into fresh test profiles. Deliberately NOT added after user discussion: arbitrary dialog dismissal — a popup/dialog is either suppressible at source (help popups), avoidable (teleport-park before long advances), or a real gameplay decision automation must not eat; revisit narrowly only if an unattended soak actually stalls on one.)*
- [x] Add the dormancy line to Phase 23's checklist (release build: property absent → zero sockets/log lines — pin with the existing dormancy unit test). *(2026-08-25, same-day as the build commits.)*

**Acceptance:**

- Property absent: zero sockets, zero log lines, all tests green — indistinguishable from today's build.
- Both instances bridged: the automated 12c checks run from Claude Code via MCP with no human input, and equality checks compare every field, not samples.
- A malformed, unknown, or throwing command returns an error response and the game keeps running.
- The manual-residue list is exactly: claim race, sell-back/hire click-throughs, motion + stealth feel.

**Deferred / out of scope:** UI-path action synthesis; screenshots/vision; input injection; save/load control; multi-client bridge sessions; any use in release builds (Phase 23 confirms dormancy, does not strip).

## Phase 31: Coop Launcher (V1 since 2026-09-03)

> **Why this phase exists (user, 2026-09-03):** "I don't expect random players to learn the flags to launch the multiplayer game from powershell." Every player-facing setting already resolves through `CoopOptionsStore` with `saves/common/coop_options.json.data` as a layer under `-D` (role keys, port, address, password, seed, port mapping, grace, HUD corner). So the launcher invents no new configuration surface: it writes that file, checks the install, and starts `starsector.exe`. The only things outside the mod's reach are JVM-level (the `coop-forks.jar` classpath prepend in `vmparams`, the mod tick in `mods/enabled_mods.json`), and those it reports rather than edits.

**Decisions (all 2026-09-03, user):**

- **V1 blocker.** Sits after Phase 23, before the Phase 19 sign-off. The public listing ships with it and `INSTALL.md` is rewritten around it, with the manual path kept as an appendix.
- **Stack: Java Swing, a second jar (`jars/coop-launcher.jar`) from the same Gradle build,** started by `Coop Launcher.cmd` in the mod folder with the JRE Starsector ships (`<install>\jre\bin\javaw.exe`, Zulu 17 on 0.98a-RC8; the mod already compiles for 17). Chosen for implementation reliability: no runtime to install, testable in the existing JUnit suite, and no second toolchain in the repo. Rejected: Rust + Slint (second build system for two screens of UI), PowerShell WinForms (execution-policy prompts, no tests).
- **Seed handling: invite string.** The host clicks *Copy invite*; the guest pastes one line. The seed stays generated locally and verified by the Phase 6b seed lock; no protocol change. Delivering the seed over the wire before generation stays a Maybe (it reorders the new-game flow).
- **Windows only** for the first release. `starsector.sh` installs carry the classpath inline and cannot be tested here.
- **Host address for the invite: IP-echo lookup into an editable field.** One outbound HTTPS request when the host clicks *Copy invite*; the host can overwrite it (VPN pseudo-LAN or LAN address). Nothing else leaves the machine.
- **Settings shown: role fields, plus a collapsed Advanced fold** (port mapping, reconnect grace, HUD corner, sector size, star age). Everything else stays on the in-game Coop Options page and in the file.
- **Install problems are reported, not fixed.** Red rows with the exact line to add and a pointer to `INSTALL.md`. The launcher never writes outside `saves/common` (and its own log).
- **After Launch the window stays open with a status tail** of `starsector-core/starsector.log`: the connection doctor block, `[COOP-DOCTOR]` lines, coop WARN/ERROR lines.
- **Connectivity checks: the host runs the mod's doctor before Launch, and the guest can test-connect to the host's launcher.** No external reachability probe (would need a probe service). Wire RTT/loss stay in the in-game HUD.
- **Pre-release additions (user, 2026-09-03):** a release packaging script (`scripts/package-release.ps1`, replaces release-checklist step 6); an update check (a Version Checker `.version` file plus a GitHub releases lookup in the launcher, one HTTPS call at start); and a random default password in the host launcher when the field is empty, carried by the invite (closes the "passwordless host: anyone ends the wait" hole from the Phase 20.2 rejoin change). Rationale for the first two: the handshake compares the git commit baked into the jar, so a stale download on either side is refused with COOP-MODS; the fix is one artifact and an early warning. **The private soak before the forum post still holds** (re-confirmed 2026-09-03): Phase 19 sign-off, then sessions with one friend on the packaged zip, then the public post.
- **Bug-report bundle (user, 2026-09-03, "Go ahead"):** a "Save a bug report" button in the launcher. Nothing existed before: `REPORTING.md` asked players to copy `starsector.log` by hand, the log rolls at 50 MB into `starsector.log.1`, and saves (which carry the campaign id and the coop DTO) were never asked for.

**Architecture:**

- **Source set `launcher`** (`src/launcher/java`, package `coop.launcher`), tests in the main test source set. `coop-launcher.jar` holds only the launcher classes; the `.cmd` puts `jars/coop.jar`, `starsector-core/json.jar` and `starsector-core/log4j-1.2.9.jar` on the classpath so `CoopPortMapper`, `CoopConnectionDoctor` and `CoopOptionsRegistry` are reused as-is (none of them import the game API; `CoopLog` falls back to log4j when `Global` is absent, which is a `LinkageError` it already catches). `starfarer.api.jar` is not on the launcher classpath; any launcher code path that links to it is a bug. The launcher sets up its own log4j file appender (`coop-launcher.log` in the mod folder) so bug reports can include it.
- **Install discovery** from the jar's own location (`mods/coop/jars` → install root), with a folder picker if the layout does not match. Checks, each a green/red row with a fix text: `starsector.exe`, `jre`, `starsector-core`, `vmparams` present; `-classpath` in `vmparams` starts with `..\mods\coop\jars\coop-forks.jar;`; no stale `-Dcoop.*` in `vmparams` (a warning: `-D` outranks the file, so a leftover entry silently overrides the launcher); `coop` in `mods/enabled_mods.json`; `mod_info.json` version equals the jar manifest's. Pure functions over file text, unit-tested; the file reads are the only I/O.
- **Config file.** Read on start to prefill the fields; written on Launch with `org.json`, preserving every key the launcher does not own. Selecting Host writes `coop.hostPort` and blanks `coop.connectHost`/`coop.connectPort`; Guest the reverse (the registry default for all three is `""`, which `CoopNetStartupConfig.roleValue` reads as "not configured"). Password, seed, and the Advanced keys are written as typed; blank means "remove from the file" so the shipped default applies. Seed must satisfy the same rule as `CoopNetStartupConfig.validateNewGameSeed`; *Generate* produces `MN-<random non-negative long>`.
- **Invite string:** `coop://<host>:<port>/?seed=<seed>&pw=<url-encoded password>`, IPv6 hosts in brackets, `pw` absent when no password. Parser and formatter round-trip tested, including IPv6 and passwords with spaces and `&`. Paste fills address, port, seed and password; a bad paste says which part failed.
- **Host pre-flight (button "Check my connection"):** `CoopPortMapper.start(port, enabled, clock)` ticked from a Swing timer until `finished`, then `CoopConnectionDoctor.hostReport(...)` rendered as rows plus the raw text. The mapper is then `shutdown()` so the game maps fresh at startup. While the host launcher is open and the port check passed, it **holds a listener** on the port (TCP `ServerSocket` + UDP `DatagramSocket`) for the guest's test; both are closed before `starsector.exe` starts so the game can bind.
- **Guest test connection (button "Test"):** TCP connect to the invite address; the *listening* side speaks first: the host launcher writes `COOP-LAUNCHER <modVersion>\n` on accept, the guest waits up to 2 s for it and **sends nothing until it arrives**. Then the guest sends `PROBE <nonce>\n` and one UDP datagram `PROBE <nonce>` to the same port; the host launcher echoes both. Result rows: TCP reachable, launcher answered, UDP echoed, RTT. If TCP connects but no banner arrives, the guest closes without sending and reports "something is listening but it is not the co-op launcher; if the host's game is already running, skip the test and press Launch." Rationale: the game's accept path counts failed password proofs toward an address cooldown (`CoopNetService`, red-team A3); the probe must never look like a proof. Verify in the build that a connect-and-close is not counted.
- **Launch:** write the config, close the listener, `ProcessBuilder("starsector.exe")` with the install root as working directory, then tail the log. The tail re-opens the file after the game truncates it and handles length shrink; it opens with shared read/write so the game is never blocked.
- **Bug-report bundle (`coop.launcher.CoopBugReport`):** zips `starsector-core/starsector.log` + `starsector.log.1`, `coop-launcher.log`, `saves/common/coop_options.json.data`, `vmparams`, `mods/coop/mod_info.json`, `mods/enabled_mods.json`, and the most recent save folder by modification time (checkbox to leave the save out). `coop.password` is blanked in the packed options file and `-Dcoop.password=…` is stripped from the packed `vmparams` (quoted values included); nothing else is edited. **Both of those two fail closed (2026-09-04):** the blanked options copy is re-parsed and asserted to hold no password, the scrubbed `vmparams` is asserted to contain neither `coop.password` nor the value the options file held, and a file that fails its check is left out of the archive under a note instead of being packed verbatim — the previous behaviour put a plain-text lobby password in a zip headed for a public forum. A save is copied only when `descriptor.xml` and `campaign.xml` were last modified more than 5 s ago (the game may be writing it). `report.txt` at the zip root: mod version, game version, launcher role, the last `[COOP-DOCTOR]` line and the last `Coop connection doctor:` block found in the logs, and the list of files packed. Output `coop-report-<host|guest>-<yyyyMMdd-HHmmss>.zip` on the desktop (fallback: the install root), then the folder is opened. The status pane says the archive contains the public address from the doctor block. Pure packing logic tested on a fake folder tree.
- **Mod-side fixes riding along:** `CoopNewGameDialogPlugin` reads `coop.sectorSize`/`coop.sectorAge` straight off `System.getProperty`, bypassing the store, so the file cannot set them; route through `CoopOptionsStore.system()`. The guest new-game banner says "seed and world settings come from the host", which is false (the guest generates from its local seed and the lock verifies afterwards); reword to say the seed comes from the invite.

**Not in scope:** editing `vmparams` or `enabled_mods.json`; Mac/Linux; a lobby browser; skipping the vanilla launcher window; delivering the seed over the wire.

*As built, 2026-09-04 (user): the first item moved into scope. Both files are now edited by the launcher, from a **Fix** button on the failing install row (`coop-forks.jar first on the JVM classpath` and `co-op enabled in mods\enabled_mods.json`), in a new `coop.launcher.CoopInstallFixer`; `INSTALL.md` section 3 keeps the manual edit as the fallback. The vmparams transform is the `Set-CoopVmParams` algorithm out of `scripts/launch-host.ps1`: back up to `vmparams.backup` unless one is already there, remove every existing forks entry in any spelling `CoopVmparamsText` accepts (so an entry in the wrong place moves instead of gaining a second copy), insert `..\mods\coop\jars\coop-forks.jar;` right after the ` -classpath ` marker, write back through `ISO-8859-1` with no trailing newline so the file stays one byte-identical line. Refused, with the manual text, when there is no ` -classpath `, when the file spans several lines, and when the install has no `jre\bin\javaw.exe` (a modded-JRE `.bat` setup, whose vmparams the JVM never reads). `-Dcoop.*` is never stripped; that row stays a warning. `enabled_mods.json` is re-emitted in the vanilla two-space shape with `"coop"` appended and the other ids untouched and in order, or created holding only `coop`. An `AccessDeniedException` (a game under `Program Files`) offers an elevated relaunch through `powershell -NoProfile -Command Start-Process -Verb RunAs` on `java.home`'s `javaw.exe` with the running `java.class.path` and `--apply-install-fix`; the elevated copy applies both fixes at startup and carries on as a normal launcher, the unelevated one exits, and a declined UAC prompt (PowerShell exits non-zero under `-ErrorAction Stop`) falls back to the INSTALL.md text. A Starsector update that overwrites `vmparams` turns the row red again and the same button re-applies; no extra logic. Tests: `CoopInstallFixerTest` (30) plus three quoting cases in `CoopLauncherAppTest`; 2614 → 2647 green.*

*As built, 2026-09-04 (user decisions, final): the launcher names the save to load. The gap the invite left open was that a Starsector save folder is named after the character and the game's load screen lists it by character and date, so a player with two co-op campaigns had to guess which save an invite was for and found out at the seed lock minutes later. `2faeb2b` gave the mod the index (`saves/common/coop_saves.json.data`) and the in-game guard (`coop.save.CoopCampaignGuard` on `coop.expectedCampaignId`); this is the launcher half of the same feature. **`coop.launcher.CoopSaveIndexReader`** reads the index, stops on a `version` above 1 rather than guessing, and joins every row to what is on disk: a row whose folder the engine has pruned (autosaves are kept 3 deep) is dropped, a surviving row has its folder's `descriptor.xml` parsed and the descriptor wins every display field it has an answer for, and a row the mod could not name a folder for is matched by `characterName` plus `gameDateTimestamp` against those same descriptors. The XML is read with the JDK's DOM, doctype declarations refused, and only the root element's own children consulted, because the real `descriptor.xml` nests a whole `enabledMods` tree inside itself. **`coop.launcher.CoopCampaignPicker`** is the pure model behind the host card's **Campaign** drop-down (New campaign first carrying the seed, then one entry per campaign with a surviving save, newest first, labelled `<character>, level N, <in-game date>, saved <local time>`; campaigns this install only ever guested in are left out of the host list but still resolve for a guest's rejoin) and behind the one advice line under each card: `Load the save "<name>", level N, saved <local time> (folder <dir>)`, `No co-op save for this campaign on this machine: start a New Game with the seed above`, or `Start a New Game with the seed above`. A missing, unreadable or newer-versioned index says so on that same line and never blocks LAUNCH. Picking an existing campaign greys the seed, its Generate button, the sector size and the star age. The invite gained an optional **`cid`** parameter, last in the query, and a parse that reads a missing one as "no campaign named", so every invite from the previous release still works. Refresh points: install adoption, `windowGainedFocus`, and game exit, run on the existing worker with the same generation-counter guard the connection check uses, because a save folder on a sleeping drive would otherwise freeze the window. At Launch the picked or invited id is written as `coop.expectedCampaignId` through the same `owned` map as the other one-shot keys, blank for a new campaign (which removes the key). Tests: `CoopSaveIndexReaderTest` (19), `CoopCampaignPickerTest` (18), 6 invite cases, 1 config case; 2678 → 2722 green.*

*As built, 2026-09-04: the picker carries each campaign's seed, sector size and star age.* `CoopCampaignPicker.Entry` gained `seedString` earlier in the same fix pass; the other two were recorded nowhere, because procgen consumes both and a loaded sector has no getter for either, so a guest who had to regenerate a campaign matched them by hand off the host's launcher. They are now written down at the one moment they are known. `CoopNewGameDialogPlugin` hands the pair it pins onto `CharacterCreationData` to `coop.newgame.CoopWorldSettings.rememberPending`, and `CoopModPlugin.onNewGameAfterProcGen` moves it into sector persistent data under `coop.sectorSize` and `coop.sectorAge`, in the launcher's own vocabulary (`small`/`normal`, and `young`/`average`/`old`/`mixed` for `StarAge.ANY`). A guest generates its sector from the invite through that same dialog, so one code path covers both roles, and a sector generated before this change or through the vanilla dialog records nothing rather than a default it was never generated at. `CoopSaveIndex.currentRow` copies both into the row as `sectorSize` and `sectorAge`, left out when the sector has neither. **The index version stayed at 1**: the keys are additive, so `CoopSaveIndexReader` keeps its `version > 1` refusal, a launcher from before this change reads a new file unchanged, and a new launcher reads an old row as "not recorded". `CoopSaveIndexReader.Save`, `CoopCampaignPicker.Entry` and the pure `…AfterPick` helpers carry the pair beside the seed; picking a saved campaign writes all three controls through the `invokeLater` and re-entrancy guard the seed already used (`writingWorldBoxes` beside `writingSeedField`), and going back to New restores the host's drafts. A row that records one of the three and not the others extends the existing hint line by a sentence rather than adding a second line, and a recorded value this launcher does not offer leaves the drop-down where it is with a WARN in `coop-launcher.log`. Tests: `CoopWorldSettingsTest` (8), 3 index cases, 3 reader cases, 8 picker cases; 2788 → 2810 green.

**Design (user direction 2026-09-03, "modern concise, dark only, in the spirit of TriOS"; done by the orchestrator, not a worker):** the first build's system-look window had zero-width fields and no hierarchy (screenshot `tmp_ff_analysis/phase31/design/current.png`). Redesign on **FlatLaf 3.6** (Apache-2.0, fetched by Gradle into `jars/flatlaf.jar`, on the `.cmd` classpath, attributed in `LICENSE` + `jars/FLATLAF-LICENSE.txt`) with the palette and factories in `coop.launcher.CoopTheme`: background `#111820`, card `#1a232d` with a `#283442` border and 14 px arc, field `#0e141b`, text `#e6edf3`, muted `#8b98a8`, one cyan accent `#4fd1e8` for the primary action and the selected role, status colours OK `#3ecf8e` / WARN `#f4a63a` / FAIL `#f0584f` / INFO `#7b8794`. Layout: header (title, version, update chip, Host/Guest segmented switch) → cards **Session** (two-column form; host: Port, Password with reveal, Seed + inline Generate, Your address + inline Look up, live read-only invite line + inline Copy; guest: invite field + inline Paste that fills the rest on change, then address/port/password/seed) → **Connection** (one role-dependent button + capsule result chips + a wrapping next-step sentence) → **Advanced** (hidden until the footer button) → **Install** (summary chip, Refresh/Folder/Guide, non-OK rows only with a Show-all toggle) → footer (LAUNCH pill, hint, Advanced, Log) → **Log drawer** (Save a bug report + Include newest save, Open log folder, Clear, timestamped lines + the starsector.log tail; opens by itself after Launch and after a connection check). Engineering facts that made it work: the cards column is a `Scrollable` panel that tracks the viewport width (otherwise GridBag lays out to content width and overflows), text fields get `setColumns(8)` so the two columns split by weight not content, chip rows use a wrapping FlowLayout, capsule arc = component height (a fixed 999 arc drew ellipses), the role panels are swapped in a BorderLayout rather than a CardLayout (CardLayout sizes to the taller form and left a blank band), notes are read-only wrapping `JTextArea`s, and the default height is clamped to the screen (860 logical px overflows a 1080p display at 150 % scaling). `COOP_LAUNCHER_PREVIEW=host|guest|install` stages the window for screenshots (dev only, nothing written, no socket). Capture tool: `tmp_ff_analysis/phase31/design/shot.ps1` (DPI-aware `PrintWindow`).

**Layout revision (user, 2026-09-03, same day):** sector size and star age moved from Advanced into the Session card with real defaults (`normal`, `mixed`) and **ride in the invite** (`&size=…&age=…`), so the guest's read-only fields fill from the host's choice instead of being matched by hand. "shipped default" entries removed everywhere: every combo and spinner shows the registry default (`auto`, `TR`, `60`) and the file gets explicit values. **Every `-Dcoop` flag the project added now lives in Advanced**: agent bridge port, wiretap sample, interaction delay, and a Developer flags group (diagnostics, datagram wiretap, frame profiler, full-fidelity guest system, disable shared fast-forward, disable clock reconciler, start over inside the host's campaign). Mechanism: `CoopOptionsStore.ONE_SHOT_KEYS` is now the whole `dOnly` set, `launcherOverrides()` lists the ones present in the user file, and `CoopModPlugin.onApplicationLoad` republishes each as a system property unless a real `-D` exists (log line `Coop published the settings-file flag -D<key>=<value>`); the owners keep reading `System.getProperty` unchanged. The launcher writes a flag only when it differs from the default; the adopt-campaign box is never prefilled (one-shot consent). Tests 2415 green.

**Build findings (launcher core built 2026-09-03, 2275 → 2375 tests):**

- **The three new-game keys were `dOnly` in the Phase 28 registry, so the file could not carry them at all.** `coop.newGameSeed`, `coop.sectorSize`, `coop.sectorAge` are one-shot gestures: not in the shipped defaults, not on the options page, refused by `writeOverrides`. The launcher cannot set a `-D`, so a narrow seam was added instead of flipping `dOnly`: `CoopOptionsStore.rawOneShot(key)` resolves `-D` then the user file and nothing else (never the shipped defaults), throws for any key outside `ONE_SHOT_KEYS`, and `flatten` stops reporting those three as unused. Both phases' intents survive.
- **A file-borne seed would not have reached the forks.** `coop.rng.CoopRandom` lives in `coop-forks.jar` on the system classloader, cannot see the store, and reads the seed as a plain system property per call. `CoopModPlugin.onApplicationLoad` now republishes the resolved seed with `System.setProperty` when no real `-D` is present (log line `Coop published the settings-file seed as -Dcoop.newGameSeed`). **Smoke must confirm the sandbox allows `System.setProperty`**: grep that line in the log of a launcher-started game, and `Coop reseeded the forked Misc.random` beside it. *(**2026-09-04:** publishing the property is not enough on this path. `Misc.random` is bound in a static field initialiser during data loading, about ten seconds before `onApplicationLoad`, so the launcher's seed arrives too late for it and `CoopModPlugin` rebinds the field from the same `CoopRandom` stream right after publishing. The `[COOP-FORK] Misc fork active` probe reading `coopSession=false` on a launcher start is therefore expected, not a failure; the reseed line is the one that says the seed took.)*
- `SO_REUSEADDR` is off on the launcher's listener sockets: on Windows it lets a second socket bind a port another process holds, which would have hidden the "port already in use, is the game running?" row.
- Confirmed: `noteFailedProof` is reached only from the `LOBBY_HELLO` and `SESSION_RESUME_REQUEST` gates with a parsed wrong proof, so a connect-and-close never feeds the proof cooldown; it does count as one of five connection attempts per window, which is why the prober connects once and never retries.
- `CoopPortMapper.shutdown()` drives its own bounded release loop (1.2 s), so the launcher runs it on the worker thread rather than ticking it.
- Engine-free classpath proven at runtime, not only at compile time: the doctor, mapper, registry, invite and seed validator ran on the game's JRE with the exact `.cmd` classpath and no `starfarer.api.jar`.

**Steps:**

- [x] Gradle: `launcher` source set + `launcherJar` task producing `jars/coop-launcher.jar`; `build` depends on it; tests compile against it. `deploy-to-test-clients.ps1` copies `Coop Launcher.cmd` along with `jars/`. `/jars/coop-launcher.jar` joins the other two jars in `.gitignore` (built artifacts are not committed; the release zip carries them).
- [x] `coop.launcher.CoopInstallCheck` (pure checks over file text) + `CoopVmparamsText` (classpath and `-Dcoop.*` inspection) with tests covering the dev-box `vmparams`, a stock 0.98a `vmparams`, a patched one, and a stale-`-D` one.
- [x] `coop.launcher.CoopLauncherConfig` (read/prefill/write of `coop_options.json.data`, key ownership, blank = remove) + `CoopInvite` (format/parse) + seed generation, all unit-tested.
- [x] `coop.launcher.CoopLauncherProbe`: host listener (banner-first TCP + UDP echo) and guest prober, tested end to end on loopback in JUnit.
- [x] `coop.launcher.CoopLauncherApp` Swing window: role toggle, fields, Advanced fold, Copy/Paste invite, Check my connection (host), Test (guest), install rows, Launch, log tail pane, Open log folder. `Coop Launcher.cmd` at the mod root.
- [x] `scripts/package-release.ps1` *(2026-09-03; refuses dirty tree, version mismatch, jars built at another commit, classloader-split violation; `coop.version` + `version_files.csv` verified against the Version Checker source: header `version file`, thread id 0 = no button)*: clean build, then `coop-<version>.zip` unpacking to a `coop/` folder with `mod_info.json`, `jars/`, `data/`, `Coop Launcher.cmd`, `LICENSE`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/player/`; refuses to run on a dirty tree or when `mod_info.json` and `build.gradle` versions disagree; `README_DEV.md` step 6 points at it. Version Checker support: `coop.version` at the mod root + `data/config/version/version_files.csv`, master file URL on the raw GitHub main branch.
- [x] Launcher update check *(2026-09-03; a fourth row status INFO was added for the unavailable case so it is neither green nor a warning)*: GET `https://api.github.com/repos/AyoKeito/starsector-coop/releases/latest` at start (5 s timeout, failure = a grey row, never blocks), compare `tag_name` with the jar's `Implementation-Version`, show "<version> is available" with a link row.
- [x] Launcher default password *(2026-09-03)*: when Host is selected and the password field is empty, fill it with a generated one (three lowercase words or 10 alphanumerics, no ambiguous glyphs); the invite carries it; the player may clear it and a cleared field stays cleared for that run.
- [x] `coop.launcher.CoopBugReport` + the "Save a bug report" button *(2026-09-03; `.bak` save copies excluded, they doubled the archive; real-layout run packed a 51 MB rolled log by streaming)* (password scrubbing, save-in-flight guard, `report.txt`), unit-tested on a fake tree. `REPORTING.md` becomes "both players press the button, attach both zips, paste the doctor line".
- [x] Mod side: sector size/age through the store; guest banner reworded; tests updated. *(2026-09-03, via `rawOneShot` + the forks republish, see the build findings.)*
- [x] Docs *(2026-09-03, plus a root `README.md` front door and GitHub issue forms under `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/`)*: `INSTALL.md` rewritten around the launcher (manual `vmparams` edit stays step 3; sections 6–7 become "the launcher does this" with the file path kept for hand editing); `LISTING.md` Installing paragraph; `CONNECT.md` mentions the pre-flight and the test connection; `CHANGELOG.md`; `README_DEV.md` release checklist gains the launcher jar and the `.cmd`.
- [x] **Game-version check (user, 2026-09-03, "Go ahead"; built the same day, 2415 -> 2445 tests; choke point = the pump's two start paths; log line matched = `Starting Starsector <v> launcher`, LAST match wins because log4j appends across runs):** the handshake compared the two players with each other but nothing compared the installed game with the version the mod was built for, and `coop-forks.jar` replaces engine classes cut from that version. Mod: at application load compare `getGameVersion()` with the mod spec's `gameVersion`; on mismatch refuse every session start with a new dialog code **COOP-GAME** (doctor line carries `modGameVersion` and `gameVersion`, remedy = install the named version on both PCs) unless the new `-D`-only flag `coop.allowGameVersionMismatch` is set (Advanced card checkbox "Allow game version mismatch", for testing a new RC before the forks are updated). Launcher: install row `Game version` read from the version line at the top of the last `starsector.log` (no plain-text version file exists in the install), OK / FAIL / INFO "unknown until the game has run once".
- [x] Launch gate (user, 2026-09-03, done `a19e1f7`): host seed generated and public address looked up at start when empty; guest LAUNCH disabled until host address and a valid port exist, reason shown in the footer; install problems and a running game hold the button the same way.
- [ ] **DEFERRED into Phase 19 (user, 2026-09-03).** Two-instance smoke on the test clients: host check (UPnP tier 3 on the MikroTik), guest test against the host launcher (both green), invite round trip, Launch both, new campaign from the invite seed reaches the lobby; stale `-Dcoop.*` row fires when a launch script left one behind; log tail shows the doctor block. *(Widened 2026-09-04 by the two features that landed after this step was written: the **Fix** button on both install rows including the elevated path, the save-finder hint on both cards after one saved campaign exists, the in-game wrong-save notice from `CoopCampaignGuard`, and that `CampaignEngine.getSaveDirName()` resolves through its `MethodHandle` on the shipped build rather than logging the WARN and falling back to descriptor matching.)*

**Commit:** `feat: Phase 31 coop launcher` (mod fixes and docs may land in the same commit).

**Manual test steps (for the smoke):** open `Coop Launcher.cmd` on the host client; Host; port 7777; Generate; Check my connection, expect the UPnP row green with the public address; Copy invite. Open the guest client's launcher; Guest; Paste invite, fields filled; Test, expect TCP, banner, UDP and RTT green. Launch on both; in each game start a new campaign; the lobby should show both players and the seed lock should pass. Then relaunch the guest with `launch-guest.ps1` once so `vmparams` carries `-Dcoop.*`, reopen the launcher, and confirm the stale-`-D` warning row. Then save on both sides, alt-tab back to each launcher, and confirm the host's **Campaign** drop-down has grown an entry naming that save (character, level, in-game date, save time) with the folder under it and the seed greyed out, that copying the new invite and pasting it on the guest produces `Load the save "..."` naming the same campaign's save on that PC, and that grepping the guest's `starsector.log` for `getSaveDirName` finds no WARN. Last, load the wrong save on the guest on purpose and confirm the in-game notice names the right one and closes without blocking anything.

## Phase 32: Shared Submarkets + Storage (V1 since 2026-09-05)

> **Why this phase exists (user, 2026-09-05):** "I'd like shared colony storage to be a reliable way to park and exchange ships (as long as we don't have direct p2p trade)." Only the open submarket is host-synced today. Storage, the black market and the military submarket are per engine, and until the 2026-09-05 fix pass a guest storage move was even applied to the host's open market. Research pass the same day (two read-only workers over `api_src` and the replicator): all three are the same submarket-cargo shape the open-market path already captures, replaces and delta-applies, ships included.

**Decisions (2026-09-05, user):**

- **V1.** Slots after Phase 31 and before the Phase 19 sign-off.
- **All storage markets are shared,** not only colonies. The 5000-credit unlock paid by either player opens it for both; each engine keeps billing its own monthly storage fee for the same contents (accepted, it is the price of one unlock for two players).
- **Black market and military submarket are in the same phase,** with the per-engine divergences listed below accepted.
- **No migration, no backwards compatibility.** There are no ongoing games. The first snapshot replaces whatever the guest's local storage held.
- **Direct player-to-player transfer stays out.** Researched feasible (vanilla cargo and fleet-member pickers exist, the mirror is a real fleet, the engagement shield already releases for the targeted mirror) but it needs a new interaction dialog on the mirror plus an escrow protocol. Recorded under Maybe.

**Engine facts the design rests on (api_src, 2026-09-05):**

- A submarket owns one `CargoAPI` with a mothballed-ship `FleetDataAPI` inside it (`SubmarketAPI.getCargo`, `CargoAPI.getMothballedShips`, `initMothballedShips` first). Storage is `StoragePlugin extends BaseSubmarketPlugin`: `updateCargoPrePlayerInteraction` is empty, tariff 0, not in the economy. No restock roll to fight.
- `PlayerMarketTransaction` fires for storage with `getSubmarket()` = storage; `getBought()` is what was taken out, `getSold()` what was left, ships in `getShipsBought/Sold`. Nothing else distinguishes a locker move from a trade.
- `StoragePlugin.playerPaidToUnlock` is private with a setter and no getter. `CoopColonySync.unlockStorage` already writes it for shared colonies.
- The interaction gate is stronger than per market: while one player is in any dialog the other cannot open one (`CoopInteractionGate.blockingClaimFor` + `applyLocalBlocking`). Storage is reachable only through the dock dialog, so host-canonical storage with snapshot-on-open and guest deltas has no concurrent-edit case.
- Black market: same stock code as the open market, unseeded `itemGenRandom` for ships and weapons (listings already diverge per engine, as the open market's do), no access gate, tariff 0, `isIllegalOnSubmarket` false. Smuggling suspicion is recomputed per engine from that engine's `PlayerActivityTracker`; a peer applying a cargo delta accrues none.
- Military: panel access is faction standing FAVORABLE only (already synced). Per-item legality adds `hasCommission()` for WELCOMING-tier items at commission-offering factions, read off the `$fcmFaction` memory string. Commission is not synced today; only that clause diverges.

**Design:**

- **Wire.** `MARKET_OPEN`, `MARKET_SNAPSHOT` and `MARKET_TXN` gain a `submarketId` field (spec id). `MARKET_SNAPSHOT` also carries `submarketCount`, the number of snapshots this open produces. `CoopMarketSync` keys stock by (marketId, submarketId). `StockItem` and `CoopShipDetail` blobs are unchanged in shape.
- **One accessor, one allowlist.** `openMarketCargo(MarketAPI)` becomes `submarketCargo(MarketAPI, String specId)` with the allowlist `open_market`, `black_market`, `generic_military`, `storage`, and an explicit deny for `local_resources` and anything else. Every capture, pre-stock, snapshot apply and delta apply goes through it. Storage uses `getCargo()` (materialise; nothing rolls), the shops keep `getCargoNullOk()` plus their pre-stock call.
- **Snapshot on open.** The host answers one `MARKET_OPEN` with one `MARKET_SNAPSHOT` per allowlisted submarket present at that market, storage only when unlocked (coop flag below). `ensureOpenMarketStocked` becomes per submarket and drives each shop plugin's `updateCargoPrePlayerInteraction`, so a shop the host never visited is stocked before it is captured rather than replacing the guest's real shelf with nothing. `CoopMarketSyncGate` resolves when `submarketCount` distinct snapshots have applied for that open.
- **Guest capture.** The 2026-09-05 open-only reject in `onPlayerMarketTransaction` becomes the allowlist and stamps `submarketId`. Storage deposits are the existing "sold back" direction (negative qty, full `CoopShipDetail` for ships); withdrawals the "bought" direction (positive qty, ship by member id).
- **Host apply for storage never drops a deposit.** A ship that fails the full-fidelity rebuild is added as its base variant with a WARN naming the member id, not skipped (today's `addMothballedShipFromDetail` skips on failure, which is cosmetic for a shop and permanent loss for a locker). A withdrawal whose id matches nothing logs a WARN and the next snapshot corrects the guest.
- **Ordering.** One TCP stream in FIFO order: a guest deposit always precedes the guest's next `MARKET_OPEN`, so the host applies it before building the next snapshot. A host-initiated re-snapshot (Phase 12c gap 2e reroll) can cross a deposit in flight; the guest's view is then stale until its next open, nothing is lost.
- **Ship fidelity.** `CoopShipDetail` gains weapon groups (slots, linked/alternating, autofire), current hull fraction, variant display name, and module variants by recursion on `getModuleSlots`/`setModuleVariant` (the 12c accepted gap closes). Officers are not carried: vanilla keeps the officer with the player on store, so nothing is lost by not sending them.
- **Storage unlock as coop state.** A per-market flag in sector persistent data (`coop.storageUnlocked:<marketId>`), host-authoritative. On every `MARKET_OPEN` the opening engine reads the private `playerPaidToUnlock` through a `MethodHandles` getter (the `CoopBarSync` pattern; `java.lang.reflect` is blocked, `invoke` is not); if it reads true and the coop flag is unset, it sends `WORLD_DELTA kind=STORAGE_UNLOCK entityId=<marketId>`, and both engines set the flag and call `setPlayerPaidToUnlock(true)`. Colony founding keeps its existing unconditional unlock.
- **Commission mirror.** Host polls `Misc.getCommissionFactionId()` once a second (string compare); on change it sends `WORLD_DELTA kind=COMMISSION` with the faction id or empty. The guest writes or clears `$fcmFaction` on its character memory. No `FactionCommissionIntel` on the guest: instantiating one would double-run salary and termination.

**Accepted limitations (documented in `starsector-runtime-limitations.md` when built):**

- Smuggling suspicion, cargo-scan odds, blueprint-learn-from-sale and trade-impact price shifts run only on the trading engine. The two engines show different suspicion strings at the same market.
- Shop ship and weapon listings still diverge per engine between opens (unseeded item RNG, same as the open market today); snapshot-on-open is what makes them agree while a player is docked.
- Both engines bill their own monthly storage fee for the same contents.
- Commission salary and bounties exist only on the host. The guest gets the access clause only.

**Steps:**

- [ ] `CoopMessages`: `submarketId` on the three market messages, `submarketCount` on the snapshot; codec tests.
- [ ] `CoopMarketSync`: stock keyed by (market, submarket); tests.
- [ ] `CoopCampaignReplicator`: `submarketCargo` allowlist accessor; per-submarket capture, pre-stock, snapshot broadcast, apply and delta apply; guest capture allowlist; storage never-drop apply; `CoopMarketSyncGate` counts snapshots. `CoopCampaignReplicatorStorageFenceTest` inverts: storage is written only through the storage path, `local_resources` never, a storage apply never touches the open market and vice versa.
- [ ] `CoopShipDetail`: weapon groups, hull fraction, display name, modules; round-trip tests on a refitted multi-module variant.
- [ ] Storage unlock flag: MethodHandles getter, persistent-data flag, `STORAGE_UNLOCK` world delta both directions; tests with a fake plugin.
- [ ] Commission mirror: host poll, `COMMISSION` world delta, guest memory write; tests.
- [ ] Docs: `starsector-runtime-limitations.md` entries above; player docs sentence on shared storage and fees.

**Smoke (two instances, folds into the Phase 19 checklist):**

- Guest stores a refitted ship with custom weapon groups and hull damage at a colony; host withdraws it and compares the refit screen field by field; host stores it back; guest withdraws.
- Guest pays the 5000-credit unlock at a non-colony market; host docks there and finds storage open with the guest's deposit.
- Both players buy from the same black market in turn; stock on the second opener matches the first's leftovers.
- Host holds a commission; guest opens that faction's military submarket and can buy a commission-gated item.
- Cut the guest's link with a storage deposit in flight; after the resume the host's storage holds it once (no duplicate, no loss).

## Phase 33: AI-Ally Battles (post-V1)

> **Why this phase exists (user, 2026-09-05):** "I'm thinking about putting these 'ai ally' battles in, but with a toggle (preferably as a panel skill, e.g. transponder) and without sharing spoils (for now)." Today the partner's mirror carries `FLEET_IGNORES_OTHER_FLEETS` on every frame, so vanilla's battle pull-in never takes it, and `CoopNpcThreatWatcher` ejects it if anything does. The 2026-06-10 design table named a fallback for two fleets in one engagement (the host pilots, the guest's mirror fights AI-controlled), but the shield was built and the fallback never was. This phase is the first slice of Phase 22 without the piloting: the mirror joins as a vanilla AI ally, and the owner's real fleet pays for what it loses.

**Decisions (2026-09-05, user):**

- **Post-V1.** First in the post-V1 order, before Phase 22, which extends it: the Phase 22 manifest later raises the ally's fidelity (officers, skills), and the KEY_STATE pilot later replaces the AI on the guest's ships.
- **Real losses.** Hull, CR and destroyed ships flow back to the owner's real fleet after the battle, as vanilla does for allies. Destroyed is destroyed; vanilla offers no recovery dialog for allied wrecks and neither does this.
- **Owner-side toggle, one per fleet, as a toggle ability.** On means "my fleet may fight as your AI ally". Default off, persisted with the fleet. The piloting player has no veto; vanilla has none for allies either.
- **Deploy everything.** The fleet AI decides what to deploy, flagship included.
- **No spoils sharing.** The piloting player keeps XP, salvage, credits and recoveries, as vanilla does for a battle with allies. `CoopRewardSplitter` stays out of combat.

**Live spike (code on main 2026-09-05; run DEFERRED by the user the same day, it opens the phase when Phase 33 starts):** `-Dcoop.debug.allyPullIn=true` on both instances creates the player mirror without the flag, stops re-asserting it, and turns the threat watcher's eject into log lines (`Coop SPIKE ally pull-in: mirror is in a battle` with sides and the pre-battle roster, `mirror left the battle` with the post-battle roster). `-Dcoop.debug.allyPullInDropShield=true` also drops the per-frame `setNoEngaging`, for a second run if the first shows no join. Runbook: `tmp_ff_analysis\ally-pullin-spike\RUNBOOK.md`. Facts to collect: whether the mirror joins and on which side, whether it deploys under a placeholder commander, what the mirror's roster looks like after the battle, and whether the owner's next snapshot rebuilds a gutted mirror. The design below assumes the join works; if it does not, the fallback is an explicit `battle.join(mirror, side)` from `CoopBattleBridge` when the engage dialog opens.

**Engine facts the design rests on:**

- Pull-in is `FleetInteractionDialogPluginImpl.pullInNearbyFleets`: it runs when a fleet dialog opens, honours only `FLEET_IGNORES_OTHER_FLEETS`, grants player-faction fleets a 700 su join radius, and never calls `canBeEngaged()` (verified 2026-08-19; `CoopFleetMirror` and `CoopNpcThreatWatcher` comments).
- The mirror is a real `CampaignFleetAPI` in the player faction with the owner's variants and per-member CR and hull, no officers and a placeholder commander (Phases 8 and 9). Player skills do not apply to it.
- After a battle the engine applies losses to every fleet in it, the mirror included. `CoopFleetMirror` rebuilds the roster from the owner's snapshot only when the owner's `fleetHash` changes (the 2026-08-19 latch), so a gutted mirror stays gutted until the owner's roster moves.
- `CoopNpcThreatWatcher.ejectFromBattleIfNeeded` runs every frame and leaves any battle the guest mirror is in. `CoopCustomsDialogStaging` clears the flag for a staged customs encounter and `CoopBattleBridge.driveCustomsDialog` puts it back as a hard `true`.
- Ability activations already cross the wire (`CoopAbilityEffectApplier`); whether the fleet snapshot carries a per-ability active bit the other engine can read is the first thing to check when building.

**Design:**

- **Toggle ability `coop_ally`.** One `data/campaign/abilities.csv` row and a `BaseToggleAbility` plugin whose effect is nothing; the state is what matters. Added to both player fleets at session start when absent, left on the fleet afterwards (vanilla saves ability state). Description states the rule in one line: your fleet may be pulled into your partner's battles under AI control; losses are real; spoils go to the pilot.
- **Owner state to mirror.** The owner's active bit rides the fleet snapshot (add one bit if the codec has none). The engine hosting the mirror writes the flag from that bit on every apply: `assertIgnoresOtherFleets` becomes a posture apply that sets the flag when the ally is not allowed and clears it when it is. NPC mirrors keep the hard `true`. The threat watcher skips the eject while the posture allows an ally, and the customs restore path writes the posture instead of `true`.
- **Battle side.** Vanilla joins the mirror on the player's side because it is in the player faction. Both directions are the same code: the host's mirror on the guest engine joins the guest's battles when the host's toggle is on.
- **Losses back to the owner.** At battle end on the piloting engine, `CoopBattleBridge` diffs the mirror's roster and per-member hull and CR against the last applied snapshot and sends `ALLY_BATTLE_RESULT` (TCP, reliable; classified in every policy table, which `CoopMessageTypePolicyTest` enforces): destroyed member ids, surviving members' hull fraction and CR, crew lost where readable. The owner removes the destroyed members, sets hull and CR, deducts crew, and shows one banner naming what was lost alongside the partner. Its next snapshot changes `fleetHash`, so the piloting engine's mirror rebuilds from truth.
- **Roster resync backstop.** After any battle the mirror was in, the next snapshot apply rebuilds the roster regardless of `fleetHash`. Covers a battle with no losses where the engine still rearranged the mirror.
